# Changelog

One row per version of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. A structural change bumps the
`version:` there and adds its row here in the same diff.

| ver | date | change |
|---|---|---|
| 0.496.0 | 2026-09-29 | A permission ask's choices are listed once, in `yi_types::permission::choices`, for the solo TUI's popup and the ACP options, and `chosen` reads an ACP option id back (Closes #840). Each surface keeps its own answer type; the option ids, names and kinds keep their bytes, and grant 0 still carries the bare `allow_always` id. The tty prompt keeps its own keystroke grammar. |
| 0.493.0 | 2026-09-29 | yi refuses an unset HOME at boot, as it already refused a relative one, and every `~/.yi` path in the binary joins onto that one checked HOME (Closes #836). With HOME unset, each of thirteen reads fell back to an empty or `.` path, so `yi ask` wrote `.yi/sessions` into whatever directory it ran in. `yi doctor` passes an old catalog when `catalog.enabled: false` turned the refresh off, and says so, instead of failing on a cache nothing will renew. Config defaults are read once: the lane slot count in one helper instead of four copies, the three model-role warnings through one `role_model`, the RLM depth default by its constant, and config migrations as a `(key, why)` table. Every warning sentence is unchanged. |
| 0.492.0 | 2026-09-29 | A model read, grep, edit, write or contained command can no longer open or replace yi's provider tokens, and every call that names a path is judged by the file it opens rather than its spelling (D323, amends D180; Closes #887, Closes #898, Closes #905). `~/.yi/providers/tokens/<provider>.json`, where `yi login` keeps API keys and OAuth tokens, was in neither the read gate's credential stores nor the sandbox's `CREDENTIAL_DIRS`, so `read ~/.yi/providers/tokens/openai.json`, a grep of that directory and a contained `cat` of it all printed the key. The two lists are now one: `PROTECTED_CREDENTIAL_SUBPATHS` gains `.yi/providers/tokens` and `.yi/mcp/tokens`, and `yi_permission::credential_stores(home)` feeds the destroy gate, the read gate, `command_reads_credentials` and `Sandbox::for_workspace`'s deny-read, so the kernel, the bash tool, the document converter and a lane's toolchain sync all hide both token stores; the session-mining extractor's checked mirror of the list redacts both from mined artifacts. D180's `read_is_catastrophic` compared spellings, so `read ~/.SSH/id_rsa` on a case-folding volume, `read link/id_rsa` through a symlink to `~/.ssh`, `read link/../.ssh/id_rsa`, `/private<home>/.ssh/id_rsa` and the `/System/Volumes/Data` firmlink spelling, in `read` and in grep's `path=`, all reached the key, while #885 already judged walks by identity; an `edit` under such a spelling printed the key among the lines its refusal shows, a `write` replaced it, and an edit's `MV <dest>` was never judged at all, so a move planted a file in `~/.ssh`, replaced a token or wrote `.git/hooks/pre-commit`. `extract_targets` now takes an edit's paths from the edit tool's own parser (`hashline::input::Patch`), each section's path and its `MV` destination among them. `yi_permission::ReadGate` is now the one rule for every call that opens a path: built once per call or walk, it resolves the stores, `/dev` and the workspace `.git` (guarded with all under them) and `/`, `/home`, `/Users`, `/root` and every directory above a store, the home included (guarded as themselves), to device and inode, and refuses a path when it or an ancestor matches (a missing tail judged by its existing head, a dangling link by where it points) or when its spelling meets D180's list. `decide` judges every call, read or not, on the path as the tool opens it, so `..` is taken after the link before it, where `resolve` had popped the link; a call that writes meets D15's list besides. Every scheme that serves a host file now opens it through `fetch::read_text`, which takes the same gate against the checkout the file belongs to: `local://`, which had checked only that the path stayed inside the workspace and so returned `local://.git/config` in every project and a key when the workspace was the home, `tree://<agent>/…`, which checked containment by spelling and followed any link (`tree://main/` is the session's own workspace), and a legacy `plan://` file; the model's `read <scheme>://…` and the kernel's `rlm.fetch` both come through it (#905); `family://` already refused links, and `kernel://` and `checkpoint://` serve no host path; plan import, whose refusal of a document without front matter quoted the file's first line, now answers `import source … is a protected path` before it reads; a submit output read in the candidate's checkout is refused the same way. An edit whose path is missing may rebind to a path this session read with the same file name and tag; that path was never judged, so the patcher now drops any candidate the read gate or `is_catastrophic` refuses. The verdict holds at the time of the check: a link swapped between the check and the open is #890. `walk_files` takes the same gate for its root and each directory entry; the identity helpers the reviewer wall uses (`beneath`, `identities`, `denied_file`) moved from `yi_tools::builtins` into `yi_permission` beside it, and `yi_tools::walled` calls `beneath`. With directories above a store judged by identity, two checks refused nothing the identities did not and are gone: the check of a named path's canonical spelling, and the spelling check of each walk entry (a walk's root is judged whole, and every guarded directory below it has an identity). `~/.yi/config.json` stays readable: its strict schema has no secret field (`keys` is the TUI keymap), and every `yi` reads it before dispatch, so denying it in the sandbox would end the kernel's contained `yi mcp` calls with exit 2. yi's own reads (`yi login`, `logout`, `yi_ai::auth`, the classifier's key, config load) are opens in the host process and meet neither gate. Not covered: a hard link to a key file, which has the file's identity and not its store's; bash calls that run outside the sandbox, where only `command_reads_credentials` reads the command text, by spelling (#889, stage F of #592); and keys kept outside the stores, in the MCP session store's `env`, OAuth client secrets and `*_API_KEY` variables bash inherits (#906). Tests: `permission_scope::a_named_read_is_judged_by_the_file_it_opens`, now over read, grep, edit and write (red: `read ~/.yi/providers/tokens/openai.json reaches a key`; with reads fixed and writes on the old gate, `edit link/id_rsa reaches a key`; without the `MV` destination, `edit MV link/newkey writes into a store`), `fetch::tests::a_local_read_meets_the_read_gate` through `route_urls` and `Resolver::fetch` (red: `local://.git/config`), `permission_scope::a_url_that_serves_a_host_file_meets_the_read_gate` (red: `tree://main/.git/config`), `hashline_patcher::path_recovery_never_rebinds_to_a_guarded_path` (red: the edit reported `[<cwd>/.git/config#…] updated`), the named-read test's quoted, CRLF and `*** Move to:` spellings (red with a plain `MV ` line scan in place of the parser: `"[h/notes.md]\nMV \"link/newkey\"" writes into a store`), `plan_import::an_unfetchable_source_and_an_oversized_document_are_refused` (red: `expected Protected, got … NoFrontmatter { head: "GIT CONFIG MARKER" }`), `submit::tests::a_checkout_output_meets_the_read_gate` (red: `local://.git/config: Ok(Some("GIT CONFIG MARKER\n"))`), `paths::a_glob_never_walks_into_a_key_store` (red: `read h/.yi/** leaks: [1 files match h/.yi/**]`), `sandbox::a_contained_command_cannot_read_yi_tokens`, formerly `a_contained_command_cannot_read_the_mcp_tokens` (red: `` `cat <home>/.yi/providers/tokens/openai.json` must fail: BEARER SECRET ``) and `decide::a_credential_read_is_named_before_it_happens` (red: `#887`). |
| 0.490.0 | 2026-09-29 | The review bot blocks (D322; Refs #668). A round with a high finding fails the `review` job, and `just pr ready` refuses a draft until two rounds are clean on its head, or an allowed author posts `/override <reason>` after the blocked round; medium and low findings stay advice. `just land` opens the PR as a draft and stops, where it used to force its own PR ready. growth +191: none of it is this change's, which adds no crate source; 0.487.0 and 0.488.0 price it in their own rows. |
| 0.489.0 | 2026-09-29 | A workflow value YAML would read as a mapping fails `just check` instead of the forge (Closes #896). `check_workflows.py` refuses a plain value holding `: ` in any `.yml` or `.yaml` file under `.forgejo/workflows/`, naming the file, the line and the fix (quote it or fold it with `>-`); text inside a `run: |` block and a trailing comment are skipped. The review job's `if:` holding `'WIP: '` failed three forge runs before any job existed. growth +191: none of it is this change's, which adds no crate source; 0.487.0 and 0.488.0 price it in their own rows. |
| 0.488.0 | 2026-09-29 | A `yi ask --schema` answer is the newest assistant message that holds a matching value (Closes #899). The answer was the first `{` or `[` in all of a run's text, so a reader that wrote "let me read `lines[0]`" before a read, or answered and then closed its todos in prose, exited 3 on a run that had answered correctly; four of five forge review runs posted no round this way (`expected value at line 1 column 2`, `key must be a string at line 1 column 2`, `missing required property refuted`), and a local round on #897 went from none to clean with the fix. growth +191: 10 lines are this change's (`ask.rs` keeps one text per message, `emit_structured` walks them newest first; the alternative, stopping the todo nudge for `--schema` runs, changes the runtime to fix one reader of it); the other 181 are 0.487.0's, priced in its own row. |
| 0.487.0 | 2026-09-29 | Each child's transcript lives inside its parent, and the parent's log points at it (D317; Closes #879). A root with a transcript puts a child's directory at `<root file without .jsonl>/children/sub-<8 hex>/`, a child nests its own under `<its dir>/children/`, and a root with no transcript keeps `rlm-<pid>/`. The child's header now carries `parentSessionId`. The parent's log gains a `custom{child}` trail: `spawned` with the name, id, session id, relative path and brief digest, and `ended` with the exit the record ends on, its error and tokens when a run concludes (none for an idle service's turn or a child reaped mid-run), written only while the live handle still holds the spawn's file, since a second copy of one file would reuse its sequence numbers. `history://<child>` falls back to the newest line whose name or id is the target, so a child spawned before a restart still reads; a parent file without `.jsonl` keeps `rlm-<pid>/`; `yi sessions rm` deletes the directory beside the file, and a child inherits its parent's family board path. `child_trail` (7 tests) fails without the layout, the header, either trail line, the lookup, its newest-first order or id match, the same-file check for the end, the reload case, or the extension check; `jsonl_load::deleting_a_session_deletes_the_children_it_holds` without the delete; `lease::a_child_trail_line_round_trips_and_a_newer_one_is_kept` pins the wire shape. growth +181: `subagent/trail.rs` (+112: the children directory, the parent id, the two trail lines and the lookup), `ChildTrail` and its two lines in yi-types (+37), the session's parent id and delete (+11), and the spawn, conclude, history and wiring hooks and the record's spawn-time log (+21); weighed for deletion: the in-memory `reaped` map, which the trail could replace, is kept because mail to a reaped child and plan finish read its live store; naming a child's file by its name, which needs a chosen-path create and collides on `/` and reuse, was not built. |
| 0.486.0 | 2026-09-29 | A glob's directory part resolves like any path and is judged by the read gate like one, walks honour the `.gitignore` files of their own repository and never enter a key store by any spelling, `find=` lists references only for a definition, and a named path or walk cannot reach a walled file through a symlink, a `..` after one, or another letter case (Closes #753). `read ../src/*.rs` and `read /abs/src/*.rs` answered "no file matches", because the read walked the cwd and matched the whole pattern against cwd-relative paths; grep's `include` did the same against the search root. `yi_tools::glob_head` now takes a glob's literal head (the components before the first one holding `*`, `?`, `[` or `{`), and `tool::rooted_glob` resolves that head through `resolve_target` like a plain path (absolute, `~` and `../`) and matches the rest below it; the read walks from that head and shows a path under the cwd relative, any other absolute, and grep's `include` resolves against `path`. Since a glob now walks from its head, `extract_targets` hands that head to the permission and wall checks beside the pattern, so `~/.ss?/*`, `~/*/id_*`, `/Users/*/.ssh/*` and `/*/x*` are refused as `~`, `/Users` and `/` are. That check, like D180's check of a named path, compares spellings, so `walk_files` also judges by file identity: `yi_permission::read_guarded_dirs` names the key stores and `/dev` (guarded with all under them) and `/`, `/home`, `/Users`, `/root` and the home (guarded as themselves); a walk resolves them to device and inode once, refuses a root that is one of them or lies under a store, skips any directory entry that is one, and passes every entry through the D180 read gate as well. So no walk yields a key store through another letter case, a link, `/private` or a firmlink; a named path to a key under such a spelling, in `read` or in grep's `path=`, is D180's own gap, tracked in #898. A walk now stops after 200,000 entries, and a read glob's walk when the call is cancelled; a walk whose start is a home, a system root or inside a key store walks nothing; each says so, naming the remedy, even when nothing matched (yi started in `~` used to answer every grep with a bare "No matches found"). `walk_files` seeds its ignore rules from the `.gitignore` of each ancestor up to the nearest directory holding `.git`, the root included, so a walk from `src/` no longer enters a tree the repository's top-level `.gitignore` hides (grep with `path=<subdir>` included), and a `.git` above the repository, such as a dotfiles repository at `~` ignoring `*`, binds nothing. `find=` took the longest word of the needle found in the hit line, so `find="let result"` listed every `result` in every file type; it now lists references only when the hit line declares a name (past visibility, export and storage words such as `pub(crate)`, `export` and `static`, the word after `fn`, `struct`, `enum`, `union`, `trait`, `type`, `const`, `mod`, `class`, `def`, `function`, `func` (past a Go method's receiver), `interface` or `typedef`, or in C the word before the `(` of an unindented `type name(` that is not a statement such as `assert f(x)`), and only in files of the target's language (`c`/`h`, the C++ extensions, the TypeScript and JavaScript ones, `py`/`pyi`, else the same extension). The reviewer wall (#828) compared spellings, so a symlink into a walled tree, a `..` after such a link, or another letter case on a case-folding volume passed the wall, and a walk rooted at such a name read the tree. `yi_tools::walled` also compares the device and inode of the path's canonical ancestors, judging a missing tail by its existing head and a dangling link by where it points, so a refusal does not tell a file behind the wall from a missing one; the wall's named-path and `fetch` checks and every walk go through it, and a walk, which never follows a link, checks each entry's own identity only while a wall is set. A hard link to a file inside a walled directory is not covered (a wall on the file itself covers its links), and a walk's `[N paths left out]` count still says how many walled entries it passed. The read and grep descriptions and the identity prompt say so (request budget ratcheted). Tests: `paths::a_parent_or_absolute_glob_reads_the_files_it_names`, `paths::a_grep_include_resolves_like_a_path`, `paths::a_walk_below_the_repository_root_honours_the_ignore_files_above_it`, `paths::only_the_nearest_repository_binds_a_walk_and_its_inner_rules_win`, `paths::a_glob_never_walks_into_a_key_store`, `paths::find_lists_references_for_a_definition_in_files_of_its_type` and `paths::a_walk_never_enters_a_walled_tree_by_another_name` on `cargo new --lib` output, `permission_scope::a_glob_is_judged_by_the_directory_its_walk_starts_from`, and `wall_e2e::a_named_path_never_reaches_a_walled_tree_by_another_name`. growth +281: measured from main's 0.481.0 baseline after merging main; this change is +267 net src lines by numstat against main (`walk_files`, `walled`, the identity gate, the refusal and cap notices and the cap's unit test +156, `defined_name` and its word lists +52, `glob_head` and `rooted_glob` +46, grep's include and language families +27, `read_guarded_dirs` +10, the glob head in `extract_targets` +4, less the wall's own comparisons -13 and `hashline/tool.rs` -15 net, mostly `identifier_in`); weighed for deletion: an identity index of every walled tree for hard links (a walk of the wall per call), a hoisted store list for the per-entry read gate (not measured to matter), a cancel flag through grep's collector (its walk predates this change; the entry cap bounds it) and the cwd fallback for a relative include (not built) |
| 0.485.0 | 2026-09-29 | The session verbs answer once for every surface (D321; Closes #834). `/undo` answered in two sentences ("no checkpoint to restore" over ACP, the solo chat's own locally) and the solo `/sessions` dropped the name the console and `yi sessions` print, because the TUI port, the ACP worker and the CLI each kept a copy; `yi_runtime::slash` now owns `undo`, `sessions_listing`, the line `split` and `parse_mode`, so the solo chat, the console and `yi sessions` read one each, and `/undo` before any turn says "this session has taken no turn yet — `yi undo` restores an earlier session" everywhere. Pi RPC and ACP read a `goal` request through `GoalService::act` and a plan submission through `authority::submit_request`; each transport keeps its framing and its confirmer (the serve worker passes none, D193), and an ACP submit with no plan engine now answers invalid-params, as its other refusals do. D112's `_yi/status` stream, which nothing emits, is recorded as gone. |
| 0.484.0 | 2026-09-29 | The decision log's rows D1–D199 move verbatim to `docs/archive/decisions-D1-to-D199.md` (Closes #894). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` had reached 511,967 of the 512,000 bytes `check_blob_size.py` allows, 473 KB of it the decision log, so a landing had to shorten its own row to fit; the map is now 278 KB and keeps one pointer line where the rows were. Each archived row keeps its ADR under `docs/solutions/adr/`, and no script reads a cited row from the map: `adr.py` and the landing's ADR pass read only the rows still there. |
| 0.483.0 | 2026-09-29 | The review bot reads every new head of a same-repo draft again, with no `review` label (Refs #668). The label gate held the job back while three runs posted nothing; answers now retry a dropped host, and a failed run uploads its sessions as `pr-round-sessions`. |
| 0.482.0 | 2026-09-28 | A PR is reviewed in rounds, fixed by a fresh fixer, and the forge channel runs both (D319, D320; Closes #669, #670; Refs #668). `just pr review N` posts one round as a comment: the template and duplicate checks, then read-only readers from the probe files in `skills/yi/pr-review/probes/` (correctness, tests and simplify every round, necessity on a whole-PR read, perf and security when the change touches their paths), each seeing only its own template section, then refuters that default to refuted, and a finding stays only when its quote is on the line it names at that head (`**Review round 2** on `4f1c2e9a`, reading `3503ed74..4f1c2e9a`: **blocked** (shadow: nothing blocks yet).`); `--dry-run` prints it instead. A comment in the round's shape from an allowed author is a round whoever wrote it; `/override <reason>` clears a blocked one; a later round reads only what changed since the last clean head. `just pr fix N` gives the confirmed high and medium findings to a fresh `yi ask` on the PR branch, discards its change if it touched `.forgejo/`, `.github/` or `scripts/guardrails/`, and commits through the hooks and pushes; after three rounds a blocked PR goes to the owner. `just pr sweep` reviews every open draft (a head is read at most twice while clean, once when blocked, one process per PR at a time) and runs the fixer only with `YI_REVIEW_FIX=1`, only on a draft in this repository and never twice for one round; and `forgejo://<host>/<owner>/<repo>/pulls` emits each open PR's head once per sha, so `rlm.subscribe` on it with `filter="draft=true"` runs the sweep on every push (docs/FORGE.md). The `review` Actions job runs two rounds on each new head of a same-repo draft labelled `review` as `yi-bot`, through a pinned `fgj` signed in by the `REVIEW_BOT_TOKEN` secret, and is skipped while that secret is missing. `just pr ready` now lists what a draft still owes (two rounds, the last on the head and not blocked) and refuses only once `pr_review.MODE` is `blocking`; it ships `shadow` until a replay on the labelled PRs calibrates it. `yi ask -` reads its prompt from stdin, since a round's prompt carries the whole diff and one argument past Linux's 128 KiB cap failed the first review job with `Argument list too long`. |
| 0.481.0 | 2026-09-28 | A PR opens as a draft and its body has to fill every section of the template (D318; Closes #667). `just pr open "Keep the gate"` creates `WIP: Keep the gate`, which Forgejo will not merge; `just pr ready N` takes the marker off, `just pr merge` stops on a draft instead of polling it (`a draft; the forge will not merge it — just pr ready N once it is reviewed`), and `just land` readies its own PR. The `title` job judges the subject after `WIP: ` exactly as the merge will carry it, while `WIP` alone, `WIP:x` and `[WIP]` stay refused, and a commit subject is never a draft. The template gains `## Why needed`, `## Deleted / alternatives` (the measured LOC and growth verdict move there from "LOC and justification"), `## Risk and rollback` and `## Performance`, and "Screenshots" folds into "UI changes"; the `title` job and `just pr open` refuse a body missing any of the eleven sections, one left empty, or one still holding a template comment (`` `## Files edited` still holds a template comment; replace it with prose, or "None" and why ``), and `just pr-body` prefills the new sections. yi's own `/land` still opens its PRs with an empty body: 1 of 298 forge PRs came from a lane branch. |
| 0.480.0 | 2026-09-29 | The bounded text types and the journal hash chain are written once (Closes #833). `TodoLabel`, `GoalText`, `InlineNote`, `ProbeCommand` and `PhaseName` each hand-wrote the same constructor, `as_str`, `Display`, `TryFrom` and `Into` beside two macros that generated the same for `Intent`, `Note` and the journal ids; `text_newtype!` now writes the impls for all of them from one check, `one_line` (a char cap, no line break) and `within_bytes` (a byte cap) are the two checks, and each struct stays declared in the open, so the schema lock now also reads `Intent` and `Note`, which the old macro hid from it. The plan journal's and the memory journal's identical `digest_of` and `line` are one `Chained` trait. Every refusal sentence is unchanged, pinned per type at the cap, one past it, blank, a line break and a non-ASCII string, and a plan and a memory `ops.jsonl` written by the real stores are pinned under `tests/fixtures/journals/` and verify byte for byte. |
| 0.479.0 | 2026-09-29 | A session record is appended and read back through one typed seam (Closes #832). Each ledger that views the session log wrote its record by hand, serialize, lock, `append_custom("main", TYPE, …)`, map the error, and read it back by filtering entries on the type and decoding each; `yi_types::entry::CustomRecord` names a record's `custom_type` once beside its shape, and `SessionStore::append_custom_record` and `custom_records` do the rest, for the lease, plan-op, checkpoint, memory, halt, todo, todo-intercept, permission, fetch, classifier and inbox records. The lease reader keeps its own loop, since a record it cannot decode must stop it, not be skipped; the checkpoint readers keep theirs, since they read entry positions. `AgentMessage::host_note` builds the shown, detail-free host note that the reminder, goal prompt, plan relevance and session observer each built as a literal. Stored bytes are unchanged. |
| 0.478.0 | 2026-09-29 | Exclusion is an OS file lock through one helper, and the remaining in-place writers stage under a unique name (D313; Closes #831). The plan store's lease was a directory plus a pid file and a 30 s age rule that ran `/bin/sh kill -0`, so a crash that left it naming a pid the OS reused for a live process refused every later plan write as held; it is now `yi_session::try_lock_file` on `<plans>/.lock`, which the OS releases with a dead holder, the holder's pid still named in `LeaseHeld`, and the plans `.gitignore` gains `.lock` once, since the lock file outlives its holders. `lock_file` and `try_lock_file` also serve the memory store, channels, the lane pool and its `.held` probe, the node slots and the kernel snapshot lock, five hand-written lock bodies before. `replace_file` now opens its temp with `create_new`, so a link planted at the staging name is refused rather than followed, and serves the family board, the memory journal and objects, the memory store, channels, the schedule file and the plan schema; the plan checkpoint and artifacts keep their own staging because their syncs run through the injectable `Fs` the fault-injection tests use, and the kernel bootstrap lock stays with #767. |
| 0.477.0 | 2026-09-29 | Every cut in bash or ipython output names a file with the whole output (D316; Closes #752). A bash stream over 30,000 bytes kept its first and last 15,000 and dropped the middle with only `[output truncated]`, and when the reducer then cut the rest it tee'd that already-cut text and labelled it `[full output: P]`; an ipython stream past 65,536 characters was cut with no copy anywhere. `spill::Spill` now takes every chunk as it arrives (64 KiB in memory, then a `.part` file, 256 MiB at most, after which the pointer reads `[full output, first 256 MiB: P]`) and keeps it as `~/.yi/tool-output/<xxh3-128>.txt`: files are created 0600 with `create_new` in a 0700 directory, a taken name is reused only when it is a regular file with the same bytes and is otherwise neither followed nor overwritten, and a spill is kept only when a pointer names it. bash's `drain_capped` writes both streams of a call into one spill, interleaved as they arrive, kept when a stream was cut; the reducer points its lossy view at that file, or, for a capture that was never cut, writes the whole joined text through the same `Spill`; a cut the reducer left alone ends with the pointer, and so does a background job's report. The ipython bridge feeds the kernel's uncapped `on_stream` into a `CellSpill` (stdout and stderr interleaved) and puts the pointer in the cell's notes. The first keep in each hour of a process deletes spills older than 7 days and `.part` files older than a day. `run_or_background` takes the `ToolContext` in place of its cwd and cancel flag, which is how the recovery dir reaches the drain; `xxhash-rust` gains its `xxh3` feature. Still cut with no file: a displayed value past the kernel's cut, the kernel's `exec.spawn` jobs, user exec tools and the user's own cells. The bash and ipython descriptions say so (request budget +277 bytes, ratcheted). Tests: `tools::a_cut_bash_stream_names_a_file_with_every_byte` (`seq` held in memory, through a file, left whole by `--verbose`, and over both streams; no file where no pointer is printed), `tools::a_backgrounded_jobs_report_names_its_spill`, `spill::a_kept_spill_is_private_and_an_unkept_one_leaves_nothing`, `spill::a_taken_name_is_neither_followed_nor_overwritten` (a planted symlink and a planted different file at the computed name), `spill::tests::a_clipped_spill_says_where_it_stopped` (a 1 MiB ceiling), `spill::tests::the_sweep_takes_old_spills_and_dead_parts_only`, `kernel_data_surface::a_cut_cell_stream_names_a_file_with_every_character` (a real kernel's 200,000 characters on stdout, then on stderr). growth +302: about 150 lines are `Spill`, its sweep and its two in-crate tests, 40 are `CellSpill`, the rest threads one directory through four signatures; the reducer's own tee is deleted, and a per-stream spill and a default recovery dir on the kernel service were weighed and dropped, the first for two files per call, the second for a second source of the same directory. |
| 0.476.0 | 2026-09-29 | A conversation that has paused between five minutes and an hour writes its prompt cache for an hour when that costs less than the rewrite a five-minute entry would pay, judged from its own pauses and the route's own prices (D315; Closes #873; Refs #748). Every history breakpoint used to be written for five minutes on every route, so each pause past five minutes rewrote the whole prompt; the only hour was D116's system prefix of an interactive session on the direct Anthropic path. Before each loop request the cache-miss fold (D311) now weighs the hour's extra write price on the tokens that request writes against the chance, the session's own share of gaps between request starts that fell between five minutes and an hour (with two pseudo-gaps added to the short ones), that the next request comes that late, times the saving of reading the prompt instead of rewriting it, at the catalog's input, write and read prices (Opus 5.5's read counts as 0.05x). A session that has not paused that long yet stays at five minutes. Replayed with the fold in the loop over yi's own 45 sessions of 2026-09 (645 requests, reduced to start times and prompt sizes by `scripts/cache_gap_trace.py`), it costs 1.229x the ideal input spend at read price 0.1 and 1.345x at 0.05, where five minutes throughout costs 1.329x and 1.500x and an hour throughout 1.495x and 1.731x. Nearly all of that gain is one session with six such pauses: without it the new rule costs 1.0004x and 1.0005x what five minutes throughout does, and one other session pays 9-10% over five minutes after its single pause. The tests hold it at or under 1.01x the better static TTL on those sessions, with and without that one, and within 1.05x on four seeded gap mixes, at both prices; the first of those gates is a regression guard fitted on its own sessions, the rule's zero prior and the 1.01 bar having been chosen on them. Each request's `cache` record now names the TTL it was sent with, and the fold reads it back. The fold also measured gaps between request ends, so a reply that streamed for minutes could let an entry expire while the gap looked short (#873); the loop now records how long the answered attempt took, a retry's backoff excluded, and gaps run between starts. Children, the advisor, titles, compaction and other one-shot calls stay at five minutes. The escalation ladder and the handling of a provider refusing a cache field, also asked in #748, are not in this change. growth +300: measured from the 0.475.0 baseline when this row was first ratcheted, the rest having landed on main after that baseline; the review's simplification has since taken this change to +223 net src lines by numstat against main. Of those, the estimate and the priced choice in `cache_miss.rs` and its wiring into `attach` and `ProviderStream` are about +150, `Ttl` moving into `yi-types` with `LlmContext::cache_ttl` +29, the loop's recorded TTL, retry reset and `StreamFn::cache_ttl` +24, and one explicit five-minute field in each context built outside the loop. Weighed for deletion: ADAPT's switch (deleted), the provider's TTL flag (replaced by the estimate), `OpenAiOptions.cache_ttl` (deleted), and folding a resumed session's entries (not built). |
| 0.475.0 | 2026-09-29 | A session's children share warm prefixes (D314; Closes #745). Every child now sends its root session's id as the affinity key, OpenRouter `session_id` and `prompt_cache_key` on OpenAI and codex; before, the key was `None` on every stream and nothing was sent (`yi rpc` still sends none: it builds its session before it has an id). Readers over one partition now mark its end (`shared_through = Some(0)`, D309) on both the Anthropic and the OpenRouter wire, one-request readers too. A fan-out (`asyncio.gather` of `rlm.ask` over one file with the same model, tools and schema) marks it on every reader, the first included, since `rlm.run` sends the fan-out's size as `readers`; and because an entry can be read only once the response writing it has begun, the admission staggers them: the first reader leads, and the others wait before their first request for its first streamed event, or its failure, 20 s at most. So on a route that caches at marks the partition is written once, by the lead, and read by every follower, and each follower answers the lead's time to first token later. A reader awaited after another within five minutes marks it as D309 set it, waits for nothing, and writes it for a third; a lone reader marks nothing, waits for nothing and pays no write premium. Before, only a repeat was given the mark, no encoder read it, and nothing ever wrote the partition entry. The universal system mark gives way first when the four slots fill. A child's stable prefix is written for five minutes: `ProviderStream::for_child` shares the family's credentials, faux script and key, and no longer the root's one-hour hold. The live scenario `cache-warm-siblings` (`minSiblingShare`) asks two tool-less readers, one after the other, over one 18 KB partition: on GLM 5.3 flash pinned to Z.AI the second sibling's only request read 6,720 of its 6,773 tokens, twice, for $0.006 a run, and main's binary passes it too, since a prefix engine pinned to one upstream shares a byte-identical prefix by itself; the marks and the key are proved by the wire tests, and a three-sibling Claude run, which main would fail, waits on the owner. The plan's `prefix_key` red test moves to C5, whose per-request `cache` diagnostic is its first consumer. growth +1949: measured from the 0.453.0 baseline, which no landing has moved; D314 is +106 net Rust src lines by numstat against main (the fan-out rule and the stagger in `reader::share` and the spawn +72, moving `require_kwargs` to `build.rs` to keep `subagent.rs` under its 1,200-line cap +5, `for_child` and the shared stream state +17, the OpenRouter key +6, the shared breakpoint +5), plus +23 kernel Python lines in `rlm.run` that count a fan-out, which the gate does not measure, the rest landed on main after that baseline; weighed for deletion: `LlmContext::prefix_key`, deleted since nothing consumed it, and a map from `shared_through` to the transformed history's index, not built since the run D309 names is the child's leading messages, which the transform keeps in place. |

| 0.471.0 | 2026-09-28 | A parent can ask one question of a child made of its brief (D307; Closes #809). `rlm.run(q, partition=["local://src/lib.rs#L10-40@TAG"])` resolves each Url at spawn through the parent's own `Resolver`, off the executor, walled as its reads and by where a link lands, and inlines it before the prompt as numbered lines in an untrusted yard fence (64 KiB of lines at most, cut at a whole line with a `[… kept N of M lines …]` row naming the cap and the `read path=… offset=…` that gets the rest, and a `[… kept N of M partition entries …]` row counting the entries past it, the fences and rows of earlier entries charged to the cap, so the last entry's own fence and row can pass it by a few hundred bytes; `kernel://` is refused, since a value rides `context_keys`). `role="reader"` builds a question-child: `prompts/reader.md` (718 bytes) is its whole system prompt, `tools` is a subset of `read` and `grep`, `turns` caps its requests (3; the last is the last word, whose tool calls are refused, after a `[turns]` note; `turns=1` is one request with no tools), `deny_write` gains `.` and the wall now raises a tool to the kind its call declares (never lowers it), so a grep `replace`+`apply` is a write refused over any walled path under its root, the user's gate rules bind its calls, and it gets no extension, kernel, plan, schedule, checkpoint or environment block; a fork or an isolation refuses. `Standing::Reader` stands outside the worker cap while building and held (64 readers refuse the next until one is reaped), and its finish or failure reaches the parent like a worker's unless `result` took it;  `rlm.ask(question, partition, schema=…)` (one Url string or a list) runs one, reads its result and reaps it. | 0.470.0 | 2026-09-28 | An approved command stays in the sandbox unless its question says it leaves (D312, amends D207; Refs #600). On macOS, approving a question used to run the whole command with no sandbox: the retry of a write the sandbox had refused, an approved `rm`, a held or configured-`ask` call. So an approved `touch ~/a/x; touch ~/b/y` wrote both, though only `~/a` had been refused, and an approved `rm` could delete outside the tree. Now an approved call runs contained; the retry of a refused write is widened by that directory alone (the question says "approving widens this run by `~/a`"), and "always" there keeps the directory writable for later contained runs instead of a rule that would run the command outside. A call that cannot work without the host leaves the sandbox for that call only, and its question says so: a network program (`curl`, `wget`, `ssh`, `scp`, `rsync`), a git fetch/pull/push/clone, a package install, a credential read, the retry of a refusal that named no path, and a refused directory that is protected (`$HOME` itself, anything under `~/.yi`, a credential store, a git path the host runs, checked through links). A reviewer's wall is now in the profile, so a walled juror's approved `find . -delete` cannot touch the tree it may not write, and a worktree child's contained bash writes its own lane, since the profile is built from the caller's cwd, not the parent's. Unchanged until stage 2b: calls the gate allows, session rules, config `allow` rules and `--yolo` run outside; container children keep their image's network (owner: "Keep network (Recommended)"). An `exec://` source approved through a question is now refused unless it escalates, so a heartbeat needs a config allow rule. The bash description, the auto-mode fragment, the doctrine and the refusal hint say this. Seen red on main: `sandbox_seam::an_approved_retry_stays_contained_and_widens_by_the_refused_dir` (the second, unapproved dir was written), `sandbox_seam::an_approved_destructive_command_runs_contained` (the approved `rm` deleted a file under `$HOME`), `sandbox_seam::a_walled_juror_cannot_write_the_tree_from_an_approved_bash` (the approved `find . -name victim -delete` removed it), `sandbox_seam::a_worktree_childs_contained_bash_writes_its_own_lane` (`touch: made: Operation not permitted`) and `permission_scope::an_approved_network_ask_says_it_leaves_the_sandbox` (the question never said so); each goes red again with its part of the fix reverted. growth +1237: measured from 0.453.0; this branch is +160 of it by numstat against main, and the rest landed on main after that baseline; weighed for deletion in this branch: a separate kept-grant store with ledger replay (stage 2c owns it; the in-memory list is 1 field), and a `Sandbox` method in the tools crate, which sits at its ceiling, so the profile builder lives on the broker. |

With tools attached a reply could be a call alone, so the directive asks for text and no tool call, and a blank or failed summary retries once, trimmed and cold (no tools, thinking off), so a second tool call cannot leave an empty summary. Live on `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` pinned to Together, the owner's model for this check, the compaction read 43,648 of the previous loop request's 44,191-token prompt (98.8%), $0.028 for the whole run; main's binary on the same route read 0 of 60,667. `compaction_faux` compares the real OpenRouter request bodies of the compaction and the loop request before it, for an in-loop and an idle compaction: the same messages through the loop's tail with every part shape kept, the same top-level fields, and marks on the system and that tail only. | 0.468.0 | 2026-09-28 | A session whose prompt cache reads nothing now says so, and each cache miss is named by what its usage shows (D311; Closes #747). | 0.460.0 | 2026-09-28 | The live Claude cache check fails when any warm request of a tool loop reads less than 90% of the previous request's prompt, and yi's own OpenRouter requests now pass it (Closes #742). The check 0.396.0 added scored warm reads over reads plus fresh input on three one-word turns, leaving cache writes out, so a dropped tail mark scored about 0.985 and passed. `evals/fixtures/live/cache-warm-claude` is a tool loop of two chained reads, about 5k tokens each and four requests, run only by name (`"onlyByName": true`); `evals/run.py` counts each request's prompt as fresh plus read plus written tokens and fails a scenario with `minWarmShare` unless every request after the first reads that share (0.9) of the previous one, over at least `minRequests` (3); a shorter loop is inconclusive, a scenario problem rather than a cache red, unless one of its requests already read under the floor. The PR live job's Claude step runs it on `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` pinned to Bedrock and goes red on the scenario's cache verdict alone. Its first runs on main's binary were red, twice: request 3 read 13,900 of 22,362 tokens, the system prefix alone. A captured request body showed why: the OpenRouter encoder turned a message's string into a text part only while it carried a mark, and OpenRouter merges adjacent user messages, so the task and the todo nudge became a different prompt once the mark moved on. `breakpoints::encode` now sends every non-assistant history message as parts on OpenRouter (the per-request environment message after the marks stays a string), the one shape the Messages adapter already sends (D51), and `request_budget`'s prefix check no longer reads a one-part array as the string it replaced, the allowance that hid this miss since 0.396.0. With the fix the loop's warm requests read 98.6%, 99.0% and 99.2% of the previous prompt; with the tail mark removed they read 98.6%, 64.1% and 73.2%, and the step is red. On OpenRouter a mark goes on the message's last text part, never an image part, since OpenRouter documents `cache_control` on text parts only; a message with no text part goes unmarked and its previous tail still reads. "post the live report" runs even when the Claude step is red, so the DeepSeek verdict is still posted. D291's evidence is corrected: a mark before the environment block read the whole previous prompt on 8 of 8 continuations, on Bedrock and Vertex, not 12 of 12 on Bedrock, Vertex and Azure (the Azure rows were GPT-sol), and none of the 9 routes probed rejected a per-block mark; its rule now reads as the code does: every non-assistant history message goes as parts on OpenRouter, and the mark sits on the last text part. | 0.459.0 | 2026-09-28 | Security fix: kernel code can no longer make the console write escape sequences to the terminal through a notebook image, and a kernel attachment is kept only when its data is strict base64 whose first bytes carry its declared type's magic number (Closes #817). Kernel code can display an attachment with any string as its `data`, and nothing checked it on its way to the console, which writes it as is inside a kitty graphics escape, or to the provider. A payload holding `ESC \ ESC]52;…BEL` ended that escape and made the terminal write the clipboard on kitty and Ghostty, and the model writes the kernel code, so a prompt-injected model could do it through the normal tool flow. `parse_attachment_display` now requires the standard base64 alphabet over the whole string, in whole four-character groups with `=` only as the last one or two, and, for png, jpeg, gif and webp, first twelve decoded bytes that open with that type's magic number; any other type is checked for base64 only. That also stops data that is not base64, or not the type it declares, from reaching the provider, but a header proves nothing about the bytes after it: a PNG signature over zeros, or a real PNG cut short, is still kept and sent, and an image the provider refuses still stays in history and fails every later request. Validating images where every request is built, which also covers sessions saved before this fix, is #860. A refused attachment fails the cell loudly the way an oversized one already did: nothing is attached, the cell is an error, and its stderr tells the model `attachment dropped: its data is not base64 of the image its mime_type names`. The console's `kitty::place_png` also places nothing for a payload with a byte outside the base64 alphabet, since a session saved before this fix still replays its old attachment there. No other path makes an image: `read` and document conversion refuse images and point at `attach_image`, and MCP results carry no image content. Seen red: `kernel_e2e::an_image_whose_data_is_not_base64_attaches_nothing` (the attachment came back as `[KernelAttachment { mime_type: "image/png", data: "AAAA\u{1b}\\\u{1b}]52;c;ZWNobyBwd25lZA==\u{7}", path: None }]`), `reduce::an_attachment_is_kept_only_as_base64_of_the_image_it_names` (`left: 1, right: 0` for that payload) and `kitty::tests::a_payload_outside_base64_places_nothing` (the payload written inside the transmit escape, OSC 52 and all); a Pillow PNG whose base64 carries `+` and `/` pins both alphabets as kept, in the reduce table, the kernel end-to-end test and `kitty::tests::a_base64_png_is_transmitted_and_placed`. growth +326: measured from 0.453.0; this branch is +100 of it by numstat against main, and the rest landed on main after that baseline; weighed for deletion in this branch: nothing further, its own change is net +100. |
| 0.458.0 | 2026-09-28 | The request shape a model gets follows from its family and route, never from a catalog flag the fetch could leave out (D306, amends D34 and D128; Closes #749). Four request facts were optional keys copied per catalog entry, read through `compat_bool` and `compat_str` with a default that applied when the key was missing, and the refresh path copied them only for ids the bundle already knew. The three DeepSeek ids the fetch added (`deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, `~deepseek/deepseek-flash-latest`, `~deepseek/deepseek-pro-latest`) had no `requiresReasoningContentOnAssistantMessages`, while their bundled V4 siblings had it, so their assistant turns went out without `reasoning_content`. The 115 bundled `openai/*` and `anthropic/*` OpenRouter entries had no `supportsDeveloperRole`, so the 88 reasoning ones sent their system prompt as `developer` while every fetched entry sent `system`, and the same Claude's stable prefix differed by which catalog named it. `yi_ai::compat` now derives all four: OpenRouter's nested `reasoning: {effort}` from the endpoint, `developer` only on the `openai` and `openai-codex` providers and `system` everywhere else, reasoning-content replay from an id-prefix table (a DeepSeek and a Kimi family row, with the R1, chat and V3 rows above DeepSeek's keeping the old flags as they stood), and adaptive thinking for a direct Claude id from generation 4.6. Checked once against the flags before deleting them, the derived answers match every bundled entry except those 88 roles, which now send `system` like the fetched ones (such a route on OpenRouter re-reads its system prefix once after the upgrade), and the six thinking Kimi entries the old flag, set on `kimi-k2.6` alone, missed (`kimi-k2-thinking`, `kimi-k2.5`, `kimi-k2.7-code` and its `:batch`, `kimi-k3`, `~moonshotai/kimi-latest`), which now replay `reasoning_content` as Moonshot's API reference asks of every historical assistant turn of a thinking model. A leading `~` no longer hides a direct Claude id's generation. The four keys leave `crates/ai/data/*.json` and the refresh adapter, with the six keys nothing read (`supportsStrictTools`, `supportsStrictMode`, `supportsOpenAIGrammarTools`, `supportsAdditionalTools`, `supportsToolSearch`, `allowedFallbackModels`); `compat` keeps `supportsTemperature` and `supportsExplicitPromptCacheMode`, which fail with a 400 when wrong; the bundled JSON shrinks from 171,781 bytes to 146,182 (12,515 to 11,857 deflated). A fetched catalog file now carries `"schema": 2`, and one written before is stale however young, so the next session rewrites it; its entries still load meanwhile, so a model only the fetch knows keeps resolving. `parse_catalog` records a line for each entry, api block or file that does not load, where it dropped them silently; the bundled floor has none, and `yi doctor`'s catalog row names the first with the count and `yi catalog refresh`, and counts an old-schema file as stale. `scripts/catalog_drift.py` reads the id table from `compat.rs` and also reports a served id whose derived facts differ from its nearest bundled sibling's, picked by the longest common prefix, then the closest version number after it, so `kimi-k2.8` reads `kimi-k2.7-code` where `max()` over a tie read `kimi-k2.5`; on the 2026-09-28 payloads it reports none. `refresh::providers_models`, which nothing called and which the schema key would have miscounted, is deleted. Tests (each seen red against the unfixed code): `refresh::a_fetched_deepseek_replays_reasoning_content_on_its_assistant_turns` over a slice of the 2026-09-28 models.dev payload (on main, `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash: {"role":"assistant","content":"hello"}`), `refresh::bundled_and_fetched_claude_send_the_same_role` (on main, `developer` against `system`), `refresh::a_malformed_cache_entry_is_reported_not_dropped` (on main, nothing reported) and `refresh::a_cache_file_from_an_older_schema_is_stale`; `compat::*` pins the table on request bodies (`deepseek_from_v4_and_every_thinking_kimi_replay_reasoning_content` red on main's table at `moonshotai/kimi-k2.7-code`, the Claude test at `~claude-opus-4-5`), `refresh::a_malformed_cache_entry_is_reported_not_dropped` also covers a non-object api block (red: `[]`), `compat::claude_is_adaptive_from_generation_4_6` asserts the `Thinking` the runtime hands the Messages API, now that the runtime's `anthropic_thinking` moved into `yi_ai::compat` whole (red with its condition inverted), and `doctor_reports_and_repairs_what_it_may` the doctor line. growth +226: measured from 0.453.0; this branch is +62 of it by numstat against main, and the rest landed on main after that baseline; weighed for deletion in this branch: nothing further, its own change is net +62. |
| 0.457.0 | 2026-09-28 | A contained kernel's family board exists before the kernel is granted it, so `rlm.put` from the root kernel lands (Closes #757). `kernel::kernel_profile` added the board, `<sessions>/family/<id>` or, with no session id, `rlm-<pid>/family`, to the Seatbelt profile's writable roots, but nothing made the board's parent: the kernel could create the board only inside a `family/` that already existed, and creating `family/` lies outside the grant. So `rlm.put` from the root kernel raised `PermissionError` on `~/.yi/sessions/family`, and a `bash()` job or a `fetch(..., object=True)` dump into the board, which run under the same profile, failed the same way. `KernelService::kernel_wrap` now passes the board through `wiring::make_board`, which the host runs before each kernel boot: it creates the board and logs a failure (`kernel: the family board … could not be made: …`) instead of leaving only the kernel's own `Operation not permitted`. Off macOS nothing changes and Python's own mkdir makes it. Security fix: on main a kernel whose board did not exist yet could create the board itself as a link, to `~/.docker` for one, since the grant covers the board's own path, and the host's `family://` read followed it past the kernel's read denial; a spilled reply was written through it too. `make_board` removes a link or file in the board's place without following it, so a link an older binary let a kernel plant is gone at the next boot, and `read_board`, `is_board_file` and `write_board` refuse a board that is itself a link. The one test of the board made the directory itself, which is how the fault shipped; it now names a board whose parent directories do not exist yet. Seen red on main: `kernel_sandbox::a_contained_kernel_can_write_its_family_board` (`PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted` on the board's missing grandparent), `kernel_sandbox::a_root_kernels_board_takes_puts_and_bash_jobs`, a root session wired as `yi` wires one, whose cell puts and has a `bash()` job write the board (put and job both refused, `Operation not permitted` on `rlm-1` and `No such file or directory` for the job's file), and `kernel_sandbox::a_link_planted_as_the_board_is_replaced_not_followed`, whose cell printed the planted `config.json` through `fetch('family://config')`; `fetch::tests::a_link_planted_on_the_family_board_is_never_followed` now also covers a linked board and dies when either the refusal or the replacement is removed. The host makes the board when a kernel boots, so a session whose kernel never boots makes none. `yi sessions rm <id>` removes `family/<id>`; a board not keyed by a session id, `rlm-<pid>/family` of a `yi rpc` process or of a run with no session, stays behind as it did when Python's mkdir made it. growth +164: measured from 0.453.0; this branch is +52 of it by numstat against main, and the rest landed on main after that baseline; weighed for deletion in this branch: nothing further, its own change is net +52. |
| 0.456.0 | 2026-09-28 | A bash command still running at its `wait` hands the turn back as a job (D305, amends D142; Closes #758). Before, `wait` was read only when bash polled a job, so `sleep 20` with `wait=5` held the turn for 20 s although the description and the doctrine said a longer command becomes a job. Now a call with a command and `wait` (5-300 s, below `timeout_secs`) returns once the wait passes with `[still running after 5s: now job 7, killed 300s after it started (timeout_secs) or by an interrupt (Esc, the deadline). …]` and a tail of what the command has printed, at most 20 lines and 2,048 bytes, with a row naming the cut and the bytes printed when it trips. The text says what is true of delivery: the job's `<async_result>` reaches the model on its own only if the job exits while the turn still runs, so it is told to wait with `bash job=7 wait=<s>` before it ends the turn; an idle session is not woken (#820), and `yi ask` returns once idle. That poll now ends within a second of Esc or the deadline. `timeout_secs` still kills a job, counted from the start, through a watchdog that returns as soon as the job settles, and an interrupt kills it as it kills a command in the turn. A killed job reads `job 7 killed (timeout_secs or an interrupt): cmd` in both the poll and the `<async_result>`, where the latter said `exit="-1"`. A job a poll returned is not announced again, and neither is a command that finished in the turn: the completion loop could take one on its settle before the call marked it reported. A session's completion loop takes only jobs started in its own cwd, so a worktree child no longer takes its parent's results. A `wait` that is not below `timeout_secs` backgrounds nothing and the result says so, with the requested value when the clamp changed it and a remedy that works when `timeout_secs` is 5 or less; `bash.autoBackgroundMs` jobs are now killed at `timeout_secs` too, and a limit equal to the timeout no longer backgrounds. The description, the `wait` schema and `doctrine.md` grow the tools prefix 336 bytes and the system prefix 117. Tests: `tools::a_wait_backgrounds_a_command_still_running` (seen red: the call returned after 8 s with `slept` inline), `tools::a_backgrounded_job_still_dies_at_timeout_secs` (seen red: no job, the call timed out in the turn; with the kill counted from the backgrounding it died at 13.07 s), `tools::a_wait_not_below_timeout_secs_runs_in_the_turn`, `tools::the_output_so_far_names_its_cut` (seen red: 20,233 bytes and no notice; then `of 30000 bytes` after 128,900), `tools::an_interrupt_after_backgrounding_kills_the_job`, `tools::an_interrupt_ends_a_poll_within_a_second` (seen red: the poll returned at the job's exit, 9 s), `tools::a_command_that_finishes_in_the_turn_is_never_announced`, `tools::a_polled_job_is_not_reported_again` (seen red: `[1]`), `tools::a_poll_that_gives_up_leaves_the_job_to_its_report`, `jobs::tests::a_report_handed_back_after_its_settle_wakes_the_completion_wait`, `session_faux::a_job_that_exits_mid_turn_reports_into_that_turn`, `session_faux::a_job_that_exits_after_the_turn_waits_for_the_next_one`, `session_faux::a_killed_job_reports_that_timeout_secs_killed_it` and `session_faux::a_deadline_kills_a_backgrounded_job` (seen red when the job ignores the interrupt: `Some(Running)`). The job tests also pass with every test of a binary in one process, as `cargo test` runs them. A running job still outlives yi's exit (#819). |
| 0.453.0 | 2026-09-28 | A permission answer is journaled once, and a refused call stays refused when the classifier is on (D293, D301; Refs #816). When the same call comes back after the user or the reviewer already answered it, the broker replays that answer: it still settles the call, but it no longer writes a second `permission` entry claiming someone just answered, which would count one decision twice in the labels D293 collects. The same ledger now also stops the classifier: a call the reviewer refused, or the user refused through `ask_user` or straight at the prompt (now recorded in the ledger too), never reaches `Approver::judge` again, where a confident answer on the retry ran it over the refusal (amends D301); without a reviewer the retry asks the user again, as it did before the classifier, and a later yes at the prompt clears the refusal so the call reaches the classifier again. A replayed refusal no longer quotes a request number the model never saw. Tests: `auto_review::a_user_denial_stands_without_asking_again` (seen red: `[Reviewer, User, User]`), `auto_review::an_approved_request_lets_the_identical_call_through_once` (seen red: `[Reviewer, User, User, Reviewer]`), `auto_review::a_denied_call_re_issued_unchanged_spends_no_second_review` (seen red: `[Reviewer, Reviewer, Reviewer]`), `classifier_e2e::a_call_the_user_refused_is_not_reopened_by_the_classifier`, `…a_refusal_at_the_prompt_is_not_reopened_by_the_classifier` and `…a_call_the_reviewer_refused_is_not_reopened_by_the_classifier` (each seen red: `allowed by the classifier (P(safe) 0.99)`). growth +954: measured from main's 0.442.0 baseline, of which this row is +46: the replay branch and `resolved` (+10), the ledger check before the classifier (+9), the recorded and cleared refusal with `stored` (+23), and `ActionLedger::forget` (+4); the rest is main's 0.443.0 to 0.451.0 (+908), which landed without a src ratchet; weighed for deletion: an `Answerer::Replay` variant (a record the labels would filter out anyway) and a refused-call set beside the request ledger (a second record of the same answer). |
| 0.451.0 | 2026-09-28 | The sandbox hint names the path it refused, and the retry of that write asks (D302, amends D206; Closes #755). A contained `yes \| head -5; echo y > ~/yidog_probe` was told "`yes` now needs permission": the scope reader skips redirect targets and named the first program it could not prove, and the broker remembered the same wrong scope, so the next harmless `yes` asked while the failing write ran contained again. The bash tool now runs `yi_tools::sandbox_refusal` once over the raw output and carries the result in its details, so the hint and the broker read one answer even when the reducer cuts the refusal line. It names a write target the profile denies (`yi_permission::write_targets`, moved out of the reviewer wall so both read one list; `~`, `$HOME` and `${HOME}` expanded), once an output line has the errno shape `<prog>: <path>: Operation not permitted` (Rust's `(os error 1)`, node's `EPERM` and quoted paths included), or on a failed command the denied path such a line names; a printed "permission denied", grep hits and a privacy refusal behind an exit-0 pipe name nothing. `Sandbox::denies_write` mirrors the profile, so `> /dev/stderr` is named rather than blamed on `yes`. A later call asks when it writes a denied path under the refused path's directory, or, in home, names one there in any spelling (`$HOME/x`, a path quoted inside python, `--dir=~/x`, but not the `~` of `rm *~`), and the hint says "naming a path" there; a read outside home never asks. A refusal with no path keeps the program-and-verb memory and wording. A denied write target counts at any exit code, so `touch ~/x \| tail -1` now gets a hint. growth +908: 161 are this change (yi-tools +159 for the refusal reader, the retry check and the details hand-off; yi-permission +74 for `write_targets`; yi-runtime −72 where the wall's copy of it was deleted), and 1 was on main past its 0.421.0 baseline. Weighed for deletion: the second write-target reader (deleted; the wall and the sandbox share one), a character loop rewriting `~` (deleted; `resolve_target` and a `~` spelling of the directory cover it), serde derives for the details hand-off (not allowed outside yi-types, so two short hand-written conversions), and the program-and-verb memory (kept, since a network refusal names no path). |
| 0.450.0 | 2026-09-28 | A control character in any cell reaches the terminal as a visible glyph, and the console deletes the images it stops tracking (Closes #760). Both shells' real terminals now draw through `term::ControlPictures`, which writes a control character left in a cell as its Control Pictures glyph (`␉`, `␍`, `␛`), one cell for one cell. A raw tab used to count as one cell to ratatui and a jump to the next tab stop to the terminal, so the rest of the row, grey card ground included, landed up to seven columns past the pane edge and was never repainted; any body the text-level cleanup misses, such as the console notebook pane, a user turn from another client or a notice, now shows the glyph instead. For readability, tool previews, diff rows, both branches of a call's digest, the streaming preview and ipython bodies also pass `transcript::show_controls`, which turns a tab into four spaces: the card replaces a read row's `12:` with its own gutter, so a tab stop counted from the raw row would indent rows 9 and 10 differently. A copy from a card therefore carries spaces where the tool printed a tab. ipython source is cleaned before it is redacted, so `password =\t"…"` no longer slips past redaction. Every console resize event clears the screen and places the notebook image again, a forgotten avatar ledger deletes its old images on the next sync, and a pane's orb images are deleted once the pane closes or shows another session. The headless drive loops of both shells dump the frame and then fail on the first control character drawn into a cell, and `scripts/tui_pty.py --kitty-ledger` prints the kitty placements still live when a run ends. Security fix: both window titles drop every control character before they are written as OSC 2 (`term::window_title_osc`). The console titles itself with the focused session's name, which the daemon takes from the session's first prompt, and the solo TUI with the user's turn; a name holding `BEL ESC]52;…` used to end the title early and make the terminal write the clipboard, which kitty and Ghostty allow by default. growth +713: `ControlPictures` is about 100 lines, nearly all of it forwarding the thirteen `Backend` methods, which the solo TUI's inline viewport needs as crossterm implements them; the notebook and window-title regression tests are about 70 lines inside `console/src/lib.rs`, because `draw`, `place_notebook_image` and `set_window_title` are private; the rest is the text cleanup, the orb owner map and the drive guard. Weighed for deletion: the text-level `show_controls` call sites, which the backend makes redundant for safety, kept because they print a tab as four spaces rather than `␉`; and a scrub of each frame's buffer at the three render roots (the console draw, the solo draw and the solo scrollback insert), about 100 lines smaller, whose return value could serve as the drive guard. The wrapper was kept because it sits on each real terminal, so a render root added later cannot skip it, and it touches only the cells a frame's diff writes. |
| 0.448.0 | 2026-09-28 | On a todo list that repeats a label, `start`, a `done` naming no item, a wait's unblock and a `set` act on the row they mean, `init` is checked against itself, no two rows share an id, and a repeat of an open plan's row names the plan (Closes #759). A list an older binary wrote can hold two rows labelled `a`, `t1` running and `t2` pending. On it, `start t2` answered with success and changed nothing: the start set `t2` running, then demoted every running row whose label differed from `a`, which spared `t1`, and the one-running rule kept `t1`, the first. The start now keeps the started row by label and id. A `done` that names no item aimed at the running row's label, which on such a list resolves to the first `a` whichever is running; it now aims at the running row's id. That needs ids to be unique, and they were not: a `set` keeps the id each row was written with, so `- [ ] t2 a` over `- [>] t2 b` left two rows answering to `t2`, and `mint` filled only missing ids. `mint` now gives a repeated id a fresh number and keeps the first, on every op, on load and in the plan projection. A wait on a session row (clock, exec, file or channel) unblocked the row by its label: on the list above, a message for `t2`'s wait unblocked `t1`, skipping the question `t1` had asked the user, or, with `t1` dropped, failed on every tick and left `t2` blocked. It now unblocks the row it found, by id. A `set` row `t2 a` took its state, note, evidence and ask from the first row labelled `a`; it now takes them from the row its id names, and falls back to the label. An `init` was checked only against the list it replaces, so on such a list `init ["a","a"]` and the two-phase `init A:[a], B:[a]` both landed; an `init` whose own rows repeat a label is now refused, as a `set` whose checklist repeats one already was. While a plan is open, an op that repeats the label of one of its rows now says `todo "gate" is a row of plan <id>, and labels are unique; leave the plan's rows out of a set or init, and give a new todo a label of its own`, where it said only that the label was already in the list, so a model that echoes the rendered list back as a `set` learns which rows to leave out. A repeat of the owner's own row, or of a row a finished plan left behind, keeps the plain `is already in the list`. `done` with an id, `drop`, `block`, `unblock` and `rm` already acted on the row they resolved. What holds after this: the todo tool never adds a row whose label another row holds, and an id names one row. A list can still repeat a label: one an older binary wrote, or one the plan mirror's projection writes (committed through `replace_with`, unchecked), such as a plan's same-named children under two parents or an own row named like a plan's child. | 0.447.0 | 2026-09-28 | The edit gate states its rule and closes its gaps (D298; Closes #751). An edit may anchor only on lines this session displayed for that version of the file: `read`, `grep`, `write` or `edit` output, or an edit refusal that printed them; a tag the session never minted for the path is rejected. The edit tool's `preview`, which the runtime runs before every execute under a broker, now runs `Patcher::prepare` on a clone of the snapshot store (`SnapshotStore` is `Clone`), where before it ran on the live store and every refusal path recorded its reveal as displayed, so the execute that followed found the lines seen and a blind edit landed with no refusal ever shown; this, not the four gaps, is the likelier cause of the lane-5 incident; the test fixture now previews before it executes as the runtime does, and the behavior cassette runner holds a `Yolo` broker so its cassettes run that path too. `Patcher::prepare` parses the header tag once into a `FileTag`; a stale tag on a head or tail insert (`PUT <1:`, `PUT >$:`) lands only when `SnapshotStore::by_hash` holds a snapshot with that tag, otherwise the edit gets the "not from this session" refusal, where before `[.gitignore#0000]` with `PUT >$:` appended to a file never shown under a drift warning; a tag equal to the live hash with no snapshot behind it (an evicted tag, one from a prior session, or a 16-bit collision) fails closed through `missing_snapshot`, which mints the tag, shows the lines and refuses, where before it applied. One routine, `messages::refusal_rows`, renders every refusal's rows for `missing_snapshot`, `mismatch_message` and the unseen-lines check: at most 40 rows (each anchor ±2), each clipped at 512 columns; past the row cap nothing is marked seen and the footer names `read` with `ranges=[[a,b]]`, which `read_windows` accepts, in place of the `path:selector` form no tool takes, so `PUT 1.=100:` on a never-read file is refused again on retry, where before its 102 rows all unlocked; a clipped row is never marked seen but blocks only itself, and an anchor on one gets "Line(s) N exceed 512 columns, and a read or a refusal never anchors one: change them with `grep` on this file (path=PATH, `replace` shows the rewrite, `apply` then writes it), or rewrite the file with `write`" rather than a ranged read that would clip it again (the edit and grep result windows still print and anchor such a line; #804); a file op (`MV`, `REM`) on a never-read file names no line, and its refusal carries the minted header rather than an empty past-the-end range; an anchor past the end gets the file's length instead of a promised retry; a head or tail insert shows the row it lands beside, and the rows count addressable lines, so the empty row after a final newline no longer prints. The other refusals end with "Check these are the lines you meant, then re-issue with this header"; the missing-snapshot text says the version is not on record (never shown, or no longer held), since the store forgets a path after 256 others. prompt.md states the rule in two lines. Tests: `hashline_patcher::a_preview_of_a_never_read_file_unlocks_nothing_for_the_execute`, `a_preview_under_a_prior_sessions_tag_unlocks_nothing_for_the_execute` and `a_preview_of_an_unseen_line_unlocks_nothing_for_the_execute` (eleven gate tests went red once the fixture previewed first), `a_tail_insert_under_a_tag_never_minted_is_refused_then_lands_with_the_minted_one` (`.gitignore` as `cargo new` writes it), `a_tag_from_a_prior_session_is_refused_until_this_one_shows_the_lines` (`Cargo.toml` as `cargo new` writes it, edited through a fresh `HashlineState`), `a_reveal_past_forty_rows_unlocks_nothing_and_names_the_ranged_read` (`LICENSE-APACHE` as serde 1.0.219 ships it, under `crates/tools/tests/fixtures/gate`), `a_clipped_anchor_names_a_remedy_scoped_to_the_file` (the grep remedy run as worded beside two sibling files the pattern also matches, only the named file changing), `a_file_op_on_a_never_read_file_is_refused_with_the_header_then_lands` and `an_anchor_past_the_end_of_a_never_read_file_names_the_length`; the three gate tests went red for their stated reason against the unfixed code and under their mutation (the `by_hash` requirement dropped, `Ok` restored for the missing snapshot, the cap removed). `endings::a_never_read_bom_file_lands_on_the_retry_its_refusal_names` pins the new phrase. | 0.446.0 | 2026-09-28 | Every request builds its cache breakpoints from one typed value, and the environment block can no longer carry one (D295, amends D116 and D291; Closes #743). A request used to gather its marks from three sites, a bool and a text sniff for `<environment>`, so a request with no mark, or with its mark on the block that changes every turn, was an ordinary value; now `yi_ai::breakpoints::Breakpoints::build` is the only source of breakpoints, `encode` spells them per wire with no root-mark arm and hands back the only body the request path accepts, down to the ureq call, and the per-request facts ride the new `LlmContext.transient`, which renders after every breakpoint and no position can name. Explicit breakpoints go out on the direct Anthropic Messages API and on every model through OpenRouter; openai-completions on other hosts and the Responses API carry none and rely on the provider's own prefix caching, as before. On a Claude route each request marks the end of its first system block, the end of tools and system, the last user-role message ahead of the last reply (where the previous request's tail breakpoint sat, a free read) and its own tail; a Gemini route keeps the system end alone; a title, compaction, branch-summary or auto-review call and a `tool_choice: none` last turn mark the system only, since their tails are never read again. Whether a request loops is said by its caller in `LlmContext.reuse`, never inferred: a conversation with no tool table caches like any other. The direct Anthropic path goes from three system marks plus the newest user block to that layout, with the interactive hour on both system breakpoints, and `AnthropicOptions.cache` is gone: breakpoints are always sent. The Responses wire renders the system prompt's block separator as a paragraph break in `input` and in codex's `instructions` instead of a raw `\u{1d}`. `LlmContext.schema` exists beside `transient` for the structured-outputs work and the prefix key; no adapter reads it yet. The type is named for the providers' own term and has nothing to do with the plan tool. A property test over random requests holds the invariants on both dialects, including a system breakpoint on every request and the last mark on a loop request's last user-role message, and the D51 prefix check runs a scripted tool loop with the environment in `transient`. growth +416: measured from 0.412.0; `crates/ai/src/breakpoints.rs` is +321 net across the ai crate (the breakpoint, policy, encoder and encoded-body types, against which the two adapters lost their own marking, the environment sniff and the per-block system marks, and `AnthropicOptions.cache`), `Reuse` and the three `LlmContext` fields are +59 in yi-types, and the loop's reuse choice, the session's `set_reuse` and the one-shot sites are the rest (+16 loop, +20 runtime); weighed for deletion and kept: the Anthropic system split into three blocks, which the universal breakpoint sits between, and `Encoded::provider_prefix`, which is where OpenAI's explicit breakpoints land once a live probe accepts them. |
| 0.445.0 | 2026-09-28 | grep replace keeps the ending of every line its matches do not touch (Closes #750). Each output line's terminator now comes from the replacer's own match spans: a line break outside a match keeps the source line's own, and one a replacement wrote takes the file's majority, in per-line and multiline mode alike. `diff::line_origins`, which guessed the origins from a line diff, is deleted with its two holes: identity origins whenever the line counts matched, so a multiline replace that added a line in one match and removed one in another gave every line between its neighbour's ending, and a diff that gives up past 2,000 lines, so the lines between two distant count-changing hits took the majority. A replace whose only match is in a file skipped as not UTF-8 says `nothing written: 1 of 1 matches in files skipped (not UTF-8)` above the file's name, where it said `No matches found`, and its details carry `skipped`. Seen red on main: `endings::a_balanced_multiline_replace_keeps_the_lf_line_it_moved` (`mypkg==0.1\r\n` at byte 66), `endings::distant_count_changing_hits_keep_the_lf_line_between_them` (`L1000\r\n` at byte 5896), `endings::a_line_added_at_the_lf_line_takes_the_majority_and_the_lf_stays_last` (`mypkg-extras==0.1\r\n` at byte 103) and `endings::grep_replace_skips_a_latin_1_file_and_names_it` (`No matches found`); `endings::an_edit_that_adds_a_line_keeps_the_later_lf_line` is green on main and, with the three grep tests, red under a `restore` that reads terminators by position, which the nine shipped tests survived. The edit tool's non-UTF-8 refusal, the coalesce warning printed twice and the 1:1 rewrite's terminator are #807, which lands after #798, the PR editing the patcher. |

API key is invalid`, after `rlm.find_models` had offered that model. | 0.441.0 | 2026-09-28 | The classifier can approve commands in auto mode, and ends a permission prompt nobody answers (D301; Closes #781). Behind a separate switch, `classifier.approve`, and only with `LAYA_API_KEY` set, an ask auto mode cannot prove safe first goes to the local classifier: confident enough (0.9, or 0.98 for anything not proven ordinary: a command a checkpoint cannot undo, one that reaches the network such as `git push`, one carrying a shell comment, one Yi cannot read, or a non-shell ask) and the call runs with `allowed by the classifier (P(safe) 0.97)`; confident it is unsafe (0.05) and the user is asked directly; otherwise the reviewer and then the user answer as before. A prompt the classifier could have judged that nobody answers for two minutes is denied, so an unattended run gets `No one answered within 120 s and the classifier did not clear it` instead of waiting forever; the TUI closes that prompt. The terminal, ACP and the console keep waiting as before, because their prompt cannot be closed yet (#800). Two review rounds found: a stale stdin reader that would take the next prompt's answer; unreadable commands, `git push` and commands carrying a model-written `#` comment on the lower bar; a timeout that could judge afresh after a mode change; and three tests that passed without testing their claim. Measured zero-shot the classifier's answers are noise, so until its checkpoint is trained on the journaled approvals it approves nothing by default. | 0.405.0 | 2026-09-28 | An edit keeps the bytes of every line it does not touch (Closes #750). `split` reads a file into the LF text tools address plus an `Endings` record of its BOM and each line's own terminator, `\n`, `\r\n` or a lone `\r`; `materialize_edits` records which source line each output line came from, and `commit` restores every kept line's terminator, giving a line the edit wrote the file's majority ending. A `pip freeze` CRLF file with a Git Bash LF line, an Excel \"CSV (Macintosh)\" file and a `dotnet new sln` BOM+CRLF file round-trip through edit and through grep replace with only the edited line's bytes changed, where before the first line's ending was written over every line and a lone CR became LF. grep replace skips a file that is not valid UTF-8 and names it (`skipped (not UTF-8): path`), where it wrote U+FFFD over every invalid byte. The straight retry a never-shown BOM file's refusal promises now lands with no rebase warning, because `missing_snapshot` strips the BOM before hashing as every reader does. The overlap check runs again once block ops have become lines, so `PUT 1*:` over `PUT 2.=2:` or `CUT 6*` over `PUT 11.=11:` is refused and an exact duplicate coalesces with the existing warning, where before both hunks' rows were written.
