The kernel preamble binds `rlm` to the module itself and `fetch` and `bash` to its functions; `_RLMCallable`, whose `result` had lost `timeout=`, is deleted, and the missing-runtime placeholder answers every name from `__getattr__` instead of a list of four. `rlm/__init__.py::_public` lists a function in `__all__`, which now carries `status`, `service`, `put`, `get`, `ls` and `list_agents`, and wraps an `async def` in `_handed_out`; the handle methods `RLMSpawnHandle.result` and `send` and `BashHandle.tail`, `poll`, `kill` and `wait` take the same wrapper. Every call still returns its coroutine, as before this row; inside a kernel `_handed_out` also appends it to `_UNSETTLED` and registers `_settle` as IPython's `post_run_cell` hook, which empties the list after every cell and runs each coroutine that never started and that no task owns to completion on the kernel's loop, printing `rlm.status() was not awaited, so the cell got a coroutine object, not its value; it ran once after the cell and returned [...]`. So `rlm.send("beta", …)` as a statement sends exactly once, `print(rlm.status())` prints a coroutine object and, one line later, the value, and nothing that awaits, gathers or schedules a coroutine is touched. Review fix: the first version of this row chose coroutine or value from each call site's syntax (`_call_sites`, `_hands_on_coroutine`, `_complete`, `_either_way`, running value calls through `nest_asyncio`). A review reproduced on 3.11 to 3.14 that it ran `cs = [rlm.send(n, m) for n in ns]; await asyncio.gather(*cs)`, a semaphore helper, `c = rlm.status(); await c` and `await (a() if x else b())` eagerly and one at a time and then raised `TypeError`, returned a coroutine silently for a method call split over lines, and let a finished call under `gather` wait for another task's `rlm.wait` to return; the reader, its four functions and `EitherWayTests` (which ran with no event loop) are deleted. The cost: a coroutine stored in one cell and awaited in a later one has already run when the later cell awaits it, and that raises. The module docstring, which `help(rlm)` prints first, says so in three lines, and `help` marks every call `async`. `RUNTIME_READY_CHECK` drops its hand list of `rlm.rlm.*` names for `all(hasattr(rlm, n) for n in rlm.__all__)` and `not hasattr(rlm, 'rlm')`, which moves the venv's name and rebuilds it once. M2's `rlm.receive()` is written as `@_public async def receive(...)` and needs nothing else; without the decorator the surface test fails. Tests: `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_runs_each_rlm_call_once_awaited_or_not` (T2) drives a real kernel against stub host handlers through a gathered list, a semaphore helper, a stored and a conditional await, a multi-line handle call awaited and not, two tasks under `gather` (the one whose call finished is not held by the other's two-second `rlm.wait`), a bare `rlm.send` (sent exactly once, after the gathered two) and `print(rlm.status())`, with no `never awaited` warning; `ipython_faux::an_unawaited_spawn_runs_once_after_the_cell_and_says_so` holds the coroutine, then the note, then the handle, and one spawn; `kernel_data_surface::every_rlm_name_the_model_reads_is_public_in_the_package` holds every `rlm.<name>` in the ipython description, identity and doctrine to `rlm.__all__`; `test_rlm.py` adds `AwaitLaterTests` (the same patterns and the two tasks on a real loop; both red on the first version) and `SurfaceTests` (every public function of the module is in `__all__`, none is left an unrecorded coroutine function, and `pydoc` renders each). growth +6746: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is -12 (the preamble's placeholder; the Python package, which the gate does not count, nets -32 at 1,059 lines after the 95-line `_RLMCallable` and the call-site reader went), and the stack of twenty-nine stages, D192 to D195, D210 to D230 and D232, stands at +18959 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`; weighed for deletion and taken: `_RLMCallable`, the placeholder's stubs and, in review, the call-site reader. |
| 0.293.0 | 2026-09-23 | The mailbox delivers every message in send order, and no run ends while a message it owes a turn waits (D230, amends D214 and D218, extends D192 and D225; Closes #493). This is stage M1 of `docs/plans/2026-09-23-the-mailbox-delivers.md`, which lands with it, taking recommendations 1, 2, 6 and 10 of the 2026-09-23 mailbox audit. Queue: a session's mail, host notices and steers wait in one queue in arrival order (`session/run.rs`), presented at every message boundary; `followup` only wakes an idle receiver, so BANANA sent plain and CHERRY sent with `followup=True` to a busy child both reach its next boundary in that order (`recursion_e2e::sends_to_a_busy_child_reach_its_next_boundary_in_send_order`, the `mbx-steer` trial), and a woken turn presents what waited before what woke it (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_turn_presents_what_waited_before_what_woke_it`, the `mbx-service` trial). Every way a run ends goes through one `settle` that, under the status lock every enqueue takes, starts the next turn when a waking message or a follow-up is owed: mail queued in the turn the repeat breaker ended starts its own turn (`recursion_e2e::mail_queued_as_a_run_stops_early_starts_the_next_turn`); an abort keeps follow-ups for the user's next turn, and a session winding down starts none. `heartbeat_hook`, `wake_idle_hook` and `notice_hook` are that one queue; `wake_idle_hook`'s retake task, `take_pending` and `run_handle` are deleted, the goal's continuation takes the heartbeat hook, and a respawned service adopts its predecessor's queue. An inboxed envelope with no presented copy in the transcript is queued again when the store is attached, once (`recursion_e2e::an_envelope_a_crash_left_unpresented_is_presented_once_after_it`). Runs: a run that starts on a concluded child record is another run; its status reads `running`, `wait` wakes on its end, a second notice carries its answer, and each run bills only its own turns (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_childs_turn_is_a_run_the_parent_can_wait_on`, the `mbx-ask` trial); `turn_ended` and `Service.reading` go, since the one resume reader concludes a service's woken turn too, and a watcher that lagged past a run's start claims the live run from its refold. Notices: a lifecycle notice for an ending the owner already read through `result`, `wait` or `status` is dropped unread at the next boundary and wakes nothing (`recursion_e2e::a_finish_the_parent_already_collected_is_not_presented`), and a finish that reaps its child says the verdict and the reaped product as one `plan_relevance` line (`plan::dispatch::tests::an_inline_childs_finish_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `a_failed_childs_last_product_survives_the_reap`). Wait and the breaker: `rlm.wait` replies `causes` beside `changed`, every envelope up moves the epoch so a cell blocked in `wait` reads a child's second message (`recursion_e2e::wait_names_why_each_child_moved`), and the repeat breaker exempts a batch during which the host's count of family waits moved instead of parsing the cell (`loop_events::a_cell_the_host_saw_wait_is_never_a_repeat`, `a_batch_the_host_saw_no_wait_in_is_a_repeat_whatever_it_says`); the plan tool's `view` exemption goes with the parser. `concurrent_notices_preserve_updates_and_coalesce_wakes` now expects the two notices at the tool's boundary and one reply, where the old follow-up queue owed a third turn. Not taken here: a stuck transition moving the epoch, and the Python `rlm.wait` docstring naming `causes` (the `rlm` surface is stage M3's item 8, in flight). growth +6758: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +199 (the stack of twenty-eight stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D230, stands at +18971 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: `wake_idle_hook`'s retake task and `take_queued`, `take_pending`, `run_handle`, `turn_ended` and `Service.reading`, the loop's `waits` and `waits_only`, and every comment this change could do without, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `adopt_pending`, since a respawn must not drop a steer or a follow-up that is not mail, and the `Cause` vocabulary, since a derived cause cannot tell mail from a live run. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.292.0 | 2026-09-22 | The five failure classes the final paid confirmation found are removed at their decision rather than reworded (D229, amends D226, extends D225 and D227; Closes #489). This is stage G5 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. Eval road: the harbor adapter's `run_command` passes `--here`, as `evals/run.py` and `evals/surface.py` already do; `yi ask` keeps its lane, since D119 and `journeys::an_ask_in_a_repository_runs_on_a_lane_and_hands_it_back` pin a lane for a non-interactive ask in a repository, and without `--here` accepted work landed in lane 0 and never reached /app (`fan-out` and `fleet-forensics` scored 0, and 1.0 with it). The todo list: a plan opened over the owner's session list keeps the owner's own rows (`todo/mirror.rs::own`), ids and all, in their phases ahead of the plan's; a row whose label the plan declares is the plan's and keeps its id; while the plan is open the todo tool steps the owner's rows (stepped on their own and rejoined, so the plan's rows never move) and refuses the rest `Mirrored`, and a `todo start` or `done` on a plan item goes to the plan tool as the owner's op (`TodoStore::carry` over `mirror::carry`, set at depth 0 in `wire_plan_request`): a pending inline todo is started and then done, a delegated one reads its standing. Children: a child's plan tool has its own description and a schema whose `op` is `view` alone, and `tool.rs::declared` refuses a child's op other than `view` or `submit` with `ArgError::ChildViews` (`not_owner`) before normalizing or parsing, on the tool and on `plan.op`; every brief ends `When the work is done, end your turn with your answer; the engine takes it as your work.` (not "submits", since `a_brief_never_names_submit` pins the verb out of briefs). Finishes: `finish.rs::fail_and_retry` fails a candidate that conflicts with the parent (`MergeFailed`), retained, with cause `its candidate conflicts with the parent at <paths>: the next attempt starts from the parent's current tree`, and a verdict whose failed items include a schema item likewise, then retries the todo as `Actor::Engine` (`check_actor` admits `retry`) unless the engine retried it before; the re-dispatched child starts from the parent's current tree. The new `plan/brief.rs::lines` feeds `ensure_spawned`'s copy of the delegation, never journaled: a todo with an output schema or a contract `schema` item gets `Answer with only a JSON value matching the schema: no prose and no fence around it.` and the schema itself (`Schema: {...}`, or its `plan://` url past 2000 characters), and an attempt after a failed one gets `Your previous attempt failed: <cause>. Its transcript is history://<agent>.`. A child is not re-driven in place: a finished plan child's run ends at `conclude`, and the host concludes again only for a service. The finish hook, finding no todo running by the child, waits out the store lease once and looks again, so a child that ends before its start commits (the engine's retry spawns off the runtime thread) is still the engine's. Declaration: a worktree delegation with no contract and a stated accept is refused in `declare.rs` with `{"command": "<shell check>"}` as the road; the plan tool's todos description names `covers?: [glob]` and a `cmd` decider as `"shell command"`, and drops `from the plan's store`, so the request budget falls 47688 to 47669 bytes. Waiting: a bare `rlm.wait` (`SubagentHost::wait_for` with `named`) wakes on a move that names a child, or once nothing runs; a reap, the engine's after every finish, moved the epoch, named no one and woke it in 20 ms; a cursor-carrying wait, the library's, still wakes on it. The `plan-covered-write` surface scenario the confirmation added is ported. Tests, each seen red first: `todo_mirror::an_owners_open_items_survive_the_plan_and_the_todo_tool_steps_them` and `a_todo_done_on_an_inline_plan_item_is_carried_to_the_plan_tool` (T0), `a_plan_opened_through_the_tool_is_the_sessions_todo_list` now carries a todo `done` to the plan (T1); `plan::tool::a_childs_plan_tool_views_and_is_refused_before_the_parse` and `plan::dispatch::a_brief_never_names_submit`'s last line (T0); `lanes::accept::a_merge_conflict_at_the_finish_fails_the_attempt_and_the_engine_retries_it` (T1, replaces `a_merge_conflict_at_the_finish_is_named_by_view`: the conflict no longer leaves the todo); `plan::finish::a_prose_answer_to_a_schema_is_retried_once_with_the_schema_and_the_error` (T0); `plan_declare::a_stated_worktree_accept_is_refused_with_the_command_road` and `subagent::a_bare_wait_sleeps_through_a_reap_while_a_child_runs` (T0); `evals/selftest.py`'s `check_command` asserts `--here`. Rewritten to pin the new rules, never weakened: `todo_mirror::a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids` (the owner's `first` keeps `t1` in its own phase), `plan_e2e::a_child_stores_the_product_of_the_attempt_it_submits` (a child's `done` carrying a blob is refused for authority before its artifacts). Not built: a same-child follow-up on a schema refusal (the finished child's run is over; the retry names its transcript instead); an automatic retry on any other red verdict; the confirmation rerun, which is paid and user-run. The G5 review (Refs #489), each fix with a test seen red first: a removal keeps its name and last listed move in a bounded list (`Children::take`, 64 kept; a cursor older than a dropped entry never sleeps), so a bare `rlm.wait` whose cursor predates a child's end wakes and names it after the engine reaps it, where it read quiet and slept up to 300 s holding the `plan: accepted` line (`subagent::a_bare_wait_wakes_on_a_child_that_ended_and_was_reaped_unseen`, T0); the mirror and `resync` project from the list under the todo lock (`TodoStore::replace_with`), so an owner-row write between the read and the replace is no longer undone (`todo_mirror::a_plan_op_racing_an_owner_row_write_never_undoes_it`, T0); a retry whose start the engine refused (a spent fuse, a contract refusal, a spawn refusal) reports `the engine retried it, and the engine could not start ...` instead of `retried it once` (`plan::finish::a_retry_the_engine_could_not_start_says_so`, T0); the finish hook takes a child named under a plan the store holds and waits out the lease and looks it up inside its `spawn_blocking`, never with a sleep on the runtime thread the child's task runs on, and a child that still runs no todo gets `plan: "<name>" <verb>, and no todo runs by it` (`plan::finish::an_early_ends_lease_wait_leaves_the_runtime_running`, T0); `plan_e2e::a_child_stores_the_product_of_the_attempt_it_submits` adds a `view` carrying a blob, refused `only the plan owner stores artifacts` (seen red with `own_product`'s non-submit arm passing); while a plan is open the todo header names the first running plan row, and `normalize` starts no owner row, since its promotion keeps a standalone list's current item and the plan's rows are the running work (`todo_mirror::an_owner_row_is_not_promoted_to_running_while_the_plan_is_open`, T0; `a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids` now pins `running: gate`). Weighed for deletion and taken, to hold the comment volume at 3600: the `Retired` alias's and `touch`'s doc lines, and two others shortened. The G5 confirmation (Refs #489), the paid run at e15ab4d1, each class removed at its decision with a test seen red first: the library's `Run.settle` writes nothing on a todo the engine owns, so a reaped child whose todo still reads running is re-read on a 0.5 s poll until the engine's accept lands, where it blocked the todo on the user and the accept then refused (`PlanError: block is illegal for todo billing in state done`, 10 journaled refusals, 3 of 4 directed tasks); only a child asking you something (`needs_you`) blocks its todo (`test_yi_plan::test_a_reaped_child_is_read_not_blocked`); the reviewer wall applies `deny_write` to what a shell command writes (redirect targets, an in-place `sed` or `perl`, the operands of `rm`, `mv`, `tee`, `touch`, `chmod` and kin, the destination of `cp`, `ln` or `install`, `dd of=`, a `git checkout`, `restore` or `rm`), so `python3 /app/check.py`, `sed -n` and `ls` of a write-walled standard run, and a refusal names the list that covers the path, `deny_write` or `deny_read` (`wall_e2e::a_bash_read_of_a_write_denied_path_runs_and_a_write_to_it_is_refused`, 11 refusals); `yi ask` holds past the last answer only on `SubagentHost::holds_owner`, a finish settling or a plan child still running or queued, never a stuck child or one the owner spawned itself with `rlm.run`, where a run held 1303 s after its answer to its deadline (`subagent::an_ended_owner_turn_waits_only_on_a_moving_plan_child`; the confirmation's logs show no record left live at the end, so the leak is not pinned to one holder, and the hold now counts only what wakes the owner); an owner `start`, `submit` or `done` on a delegated todo in a plan the engine closed at its last accept reads its standing (`done: the engine accepted it`) instead of `no plan is open` or `not active` (`declare.rs::owner_root`, `plan::finish::an_owner_done_after_the_engine_closed_the_plan_names_the_acceptance`); a `todo set`, `init` or `append` with a plan open writes the owner's rows beside the plan's, their ids minted past the plan's, where the prelude that fired before `plan init` led to a `Mirrored` refusal (`todo_mirror::a_todo_set_or_init_under_a_plan_writes_the_owners_rows_beside_it`); a todo id followed by its label, cut or the whole request line it was cut from, names its item (`todo_e2e::a_label_the_renderer_cut_names_its_item_back`). Not taken: reordering the engine's accept notice ahead of the next tool result (the owner's step now carries the acceptance in its own result); a plan `set` row past 80 characters stays refused with its measure, as #472 decided and `plan_ops::an_over_long_set_label_is_measured_not_called_a_bad_row` pins; a sync `rlm.status()`, since a host reply needs the kernel's loop the cell holds, and the un-awaited coroutine hint already fires. Weighed for deletion and taken, to hold the comment volume at 3600: three `wall.rs` doc comments shortened to one line. Review fix (Refs #489): `ask.rs::follow` waits at most until the deadline on every receive, not only while holding (`ask::tests::a_steered_turn_past_the_deadline_ends_the_run`); `Children::take` records the removal at the reap's own epoch (`subagent::a_bare_wait_wakes_once_on_the_reap_of_a_child_it_saw_finish`); the finish hook touches the child once the engine settled it (`finish::tests::a_finish_the_engine_left_running_moves_the_family`); `Run.settle` arms the 0.5 s re-read for a finished or failed child whose todo is still running and not left, and `fake_host` gains `settles_after`, a host whose wait blocks like the real one while the engine settles later (`test_a_finish_the_engine_settles_later_is_read_on_a_short_poll`). growth +6415: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +712 and the review fixes across D224 to D229 are +175 (the stack of twenty-seven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D229, stands at +18628 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: a `wait_named` wrapper, the doc lines of `own`, `rejoin`, `carry` and `CarryFn`, and the stale "any family move wakes a waiter" line, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `mirror::rejoin`, since stepping the whole list would let `normalize` demote the plan's running rows. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.291.0 | 2026-09-22 | A write to a path a running todo's contract covers runs the contract's `cmd` items on a copy of the writer's tree and the write result carries the verdict: a preview, never an acceptance (D228, extends D194; Closes #488). This is stage G4 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. `Contract` gains `covers`, globs relative to the checkout, skipped when empty so every contract frozen before it keeps its digest (`schemas.lock` moves in its own ratchet). The new `plan/covers.rs::PlanEngine::preview` reads the todos running by the writing actor, an owner's inline todos (`main`) or a child's own todo, whose contract covers a written path (`globset` with `literal_separator`, already a workspace dependency, now named by `yi-runtime`), captures the writer's checkout (a child's worktree, not the parent checkout its engine stands on) with the engine's snapshotter, or a shadow tree or a content hash for another checkout, uncommitted bytes included, materializes it into a temp workspace as `done`'s verifier does, runs each `cmd` item's frozen manifest there through `verify.rs::run_cmd` under `protecting`, so a checker that rewrites a protected path fails and nothing it writes reaches the live tree, under the item's and the manifest's timeout, capped at `DEFAULT_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, the session deadline and `PREVIEW_BUDGET_MS` (60 s across every item of every covering todo), and stopped, process group and all, by the tool call's cancel flag, and answers `contract "alpha" check: pass` or `contract "alpha" check: fail: exit 1: <tail>`; a pass or fail is kept per plan, todo, attempt, item and checker digest against the captured tree id, so a save that leaves the tree unchanged runs nothing and a file created beside it runs the item again; an abstention is never kept. It journals nothing, mints no token and charges no refusal. `ToolAdapter` gains a check slot the session fills from its plan service's engine (`covers::write_check`, a weak hold, at every depth, since a child's engine reads the same plans directory); a write-kind call that did not error has its targets (`permission::extract_targets`, so an `edit` patch names each section's path) checked on its own blocking thread and the line appended. The slot is read from the existing plan service rather than a new session slot, since `session.rs` stands at the 1,200 cap, and `preview` takes the written paths, not one path, since an edit may touch several. No tool description changes, so the request budget is flat; no doctrine line told the model to run the check after a save, so none is deleted. The G3 review's findings are folded in, each with a test seen red first: `Run.settle` collects a finished child's todo only once the engine says it left it, since `view` now names `the engine left "alpha" running: <why>` for a running delegated todo whose finish the engine left (`schedule.rs::left_to_owner`), so a finish still in flight is waited on and a red verdict landing after another child's end is retried; the mirrored todo list re-reads its plan (`TodoStore::resync` over `Mirror::resync`) before every todo op, at every prompt and at wiring, so a plan another engine finished, or a crash between the commit and the replace, no longer holds the list; a mirrored child's id is looked up under its own parent, so `api > tests` and `cli > tests` keep two ids; `import` refuses an active plan beside an open one with `PlanExists`. The G2b review's findings are folded in the same way: `rlm.wait` advances the bare cursor only from bare calls, and `RLMSubagent.result`, `Todo.result` and `Run` start at cursor 0, so a fresh waiter on a child that already finished answers at once; the directed `fan-out`, `toolbox-port` and `fleet-forensics` instructions name relative acceptance commands, since the contract runs in the child's checkout; the repeat breaker exempts only a batch of waits (an `ipython` cell whose lines are `rlm.wait(`, `asyncio.sleep(` or `.result(` calls, block headers, `break`, `continue`, `pass` or a `print` of a bare name, with at least one wait, or a plan `view`) while a child is live; an accept-derived contract item carries `DEFAULT_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`; an owner step on a todo whose child the host no longer holds takes the normal road; a blob that cannot be stored after the commit is a notice and the scheduling pass still runs; `conclude` holds `settling` from before the exit is visible, and `yi ask` ends its hold at `--deadline`; `read` of a url passes its `offset` and `limit` as a page, refuses a `limit` of 0, and names the next offset with the page's unit (bytes for `local://`, entries for `history://`, chars for `kernel://`); `done.rs`'s lease comment no longer claims every other op refuses at once. The D227 row names the bare cursor and the batch of waits. The G4 review's findings are folded in, each with a test seen red first (Refs #488): a finish the engine leaves running, on a refused verdict, a merge conflict, an unserved product, a verification that could not run or a refused `fail`, journals an engine `left` record (`state.rs::KIND_LEFT`, a reducer no-op) with its detail, and `left_to_owner` reads that record alone, since a merge conflict journals only a disposition and the others journal nothing, which left `Run.settle` waiting out its budget; `view` and `plan.op` return `left` and `unstarted` maps of label to reason (`schedule.rs::Standing`), and the library reads those instead of matching the notice prose, which quotes a label with `{:?}` so a label holding `"` or `\` never matched; the preview runs on a copy of the tree, under a 60 s budget and the caller's cancel, and its verdicts are keyed by the tree, as above; the owner's preview reads the open root alone (`resolve`), not every root in the store. Tests: `contract::a_contract_without_covers_keeps_its_digest` (T0, yi-types); `plan_ops::contracts::a_covered_write_runs_the_cmd_item_and_debounces` (T0, a `write` through `ToolAdapter`; an unchanged save over a tree that gained `src/ok` runs again), `an_uncovered_write_gets_no_line`, `a_child_sees_only_its_own_todos_verdict` (the child's own checkout holds the marker), `a_preview_writes_no_journal_record`, `a_preview_leaves_the_tree_unchanged_and_guards_protected_paths`, `a_preview_stops_on_cancel_and_at_the_deadline` and `a_finish_the_engine_left_running_is_named_by_view` (an abstention and an unserved product) (T0); `lanes::accept::a_merge_conflict_at_the_finish_is_named_by_view` (T1); `loop_events::only_a_cell_of_wait_calls_and_control_flow_is_a_wait` (T0); `test_yi_plan::test_what_the_engine_left_is_read_by_label_not_by_its_prose` (T0, library); `todo_mirror::a_list_whose_plan_another_engine_finished_is_released` and `a_child_label_under_two_parents_keeps_two_ids`, `plan_import::an_active_import_beside_an_open_plan_is_refused`, `subagent::a_cursor_carrying_wait_leaves_the_bare_cursor_alone` and `a_finishing_child_keeps_the_host_busy_from_its_exit`, `loop_events::a_repeated_call_that_is_not_a_wait_is_broken_while_work_is_live`, `plan_declare::an_accept_command_keeps_the_check_timeout`, `an_owner_step_on_a_todo_whose_child_is_gone_takes_the_normal_road` and `a_blob_that_cannot_be_stored_leaves_the_op_recorded_and_scheduled`, `fetch_session::a_url_read_through_the_read_tool_keeps_its_window` and `yi ask`'s `a_hold_ends_at_the_deadline` (T0, new); `test_yi_shapes::test_a_finish_still_in_flight_is_waited_on_and_its_refusal_retried` and `test_rlm_handle::test_a_fresh_waiter_on_a_finished_child_answers_at_once` (T0, library). Rewritten to pin the new rules, never weakened: `every_surface_requires_matching_verified_completion`'s clone is a finished plan; `plan_recovery::a_checkpoint_without_its_journal_is_named_and_adopted_by_import` is refused beside the open plan and adopts once that plan is dropped; the fuzzer's import model admits the refusal; `a_repeated_batch_while_launched_work_is_live_is_a_wait` sends an `rlm.wait` cell; the first cursor `test_rlm` and `test_rlm_handle` read, and the scheduler's first in `test_yi_shapes`, is 0, and no later scheduler wait reads from 0; `fetch_session::a_url_read_through_the_read_tool_keeps_its_window` names the unit and refuses `limit: 0`. Not taken: projecting the mirror from committed state, since it is cost only and moves nine commit sites in files at the cap; storing blobs before the commit, since an init's plan id is allocated inside the op. Not built: the `edit-file` confirmation with a `covers` contract the plan names, which is paid and user-run; the eval fixtures' own changes have no test. Review fix (Refs #488): `covers.rs::preview_deadline` is the run's deadline, and `covers::tests::a_preview_gets_one_minute_or_the_session_deadline` goes red if `PREVIEW_BUDGET_MS` moves. growth +5528: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +537 (the stack of twenty-six stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D228, stands at +17741 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: every comment this change could do without, to hold the comment volume at 3600, and `done.rs`'s false lease clause; weighed and kept: `yi ask`'s `holds`, one line with its own test. No new crate, env var or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.290.0 | 2026-09-22 | The five failure classes the paid G2 confirmation found are removed at their decision rather than reworded (D227, extends D223 and D225; Closes #487). This is stage G2b of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. Declaration: the new `plan/declare.rs::normalize` runs before the parse on the plan tool and on `plan.op`. A `cmd` item's checker written as the shell command it is becomes a checker manifest the host builds (`snapshot_root`, no protected paths, 60 s like the library's `cmd()`) and names by digest; a worktree delegation with no `contract` whose `accept` is `{command}` gets that command as its one critical `writer` item, so D223's rule still holds; an `isolation` beside `spec` moves into it (a different second value is refused), `delegate` reads as `delegation` and a todo-level `accept` as the delegation's. The blobs land in the plan's store after the op commits and before the scheduling pass (`PlanEngine::apply_with`). In the library `Writer(accept="make -s check")` is one critical command. Waiting: `rlm.wait` with no cursor uses the caller's last one, so it blocks until a child moves; `LoopConfig::waiting`, the todo coupling's live-children read, exempts a repeated batch from the repeat breaker while a child is live or its finish is settling (`SubagentHost::busy` counts finishes in flight); `yi ask`, now `crates/cli/src/ask.rs`, holds the run open at an `AgentEnd` while the host is busy and ends once it is idle and no turn followed. Lanes: the engine opens its lane pool at the first checkout that needs one (`with_lane_home`), so a repository initialised mid-session gets its candidates verified; a candidate commit leaves `__pycache__`, `*.pyc` and `.pytest_cache` out and commits nothing when that is all there is; `evals/surface.py` seeds its workspace as a git repository. Owner steps: an owner `start` on a delegated todo, and a `submit` or `done` on one when the finish hook is installed (`Delegate::finishes`), answers `nothing to do: the engine starts delegated todos; "alpha" is running by <agent>; ...` and journals nothing, unless the plan is not active or a spawn intent is pending; the `plan-fanout` and `plan-child-submit` scenarios and the directed instructions no longer tell the owner to step, and `fan-out` gains a directed instruction and a git repository. Briefs: a delegation note over 1024 bytes is stored whole as `text/markdown` in the plan's store, the note keeps its head and a `note_ref`, and the brief says `read plan://<plan>/artifacts/<hex>`; the library stores the same bytes itself, so a redeclaration is no drift; `read` resolves any `scheme://` address through the session's resolver (`fetch/read.rs::route_urls`). Also: `view` shows the plan written last when none is open; the phase refusal reads `it needs a candidate_submitted record on this attempt`; `history://<plan>/<todo>/tail/1` finds the agent by the longest prefix; an op waits out a short lease hold instead of refusing. Folds in the G3 review: the finish hook runs a delegation's accept command only for a todo with no contract that is not a worktree todo, whose verifier decides instead, and runs it in the host's checkout (`goal::run_check_at`), not the process's cwd (`plan::finish::an_accept_command_runs_in_the_childs_checkout`, `a_contracted_delegations_accept_command_is_not_run`); a fenced result's discoveries are read through `json_answer` and routed (`a_fenced_answer_still_routes_its_discoveries`). Review fix (Refs #487): `contracts::a_retry_swapping_in_an_uncontracted_worktree_delegation_is_refused` also parses the uncontracted worktree spec through `TodoSpec`'s `try_from` and asserts the `UNCONTRACTED_WORKTREE` refusal, so the journey dies with either check. growth +4991: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +679 (the stack of twenty-five stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D227, stands at +17204 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: thirty-one comment lines of this change to keep the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `yi ask`'s poll while held, since a steer that lands between two runs starts none. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.289.0 | 2026-09-22 | With a plan open the session todo list is the plan's view: there is one list, and the todo tool no longer steps it (D226, amends D137; Closes #486). This is stage G3 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. The new `todo/mirror.rs::Mirror` wraps the depth-0 engine's `OpSink` (the `TodoStore` is now built in `wire_plan_request`, before the engine): after the inner sink records an op it reads the op's root plan and hands `TodoStore::replace` the projection, one phase named by the plan id and one item per todo with its children, the todo's state, a blocked note or a fail cause as `note`, `extra.plan`, and `extra.by` while it runs; an item keeps the `t{n}` id its label already had and a new label takes the next. `replace` records a `custom{todo}` entry with op `plan` and actor `engine` and runs the change hooks, so the environment block's `todos:` line, ACP's `_yi/todo`, the TUI and console HUD and `yi todo` show the plan with no reader changed; an unchanged projection records nothing. While the plan is active the list carries `extra.plan`, and `TodoStore::apply_as` refuses every state-changing op on it with `TodoError::Mirrored`: "the list is plan {plan}; the plan tool changes it (append, block, drop, start and done on your own todos; the engine steps delegated todos)", one guard for the tool, the seed prelude and ACP; `view` passes. The todo tool does not forward to plan ops: a second road to one op teaches two. A mirrored list offers no `next:` todo moves, a failed item renders `(failed: <cause>)`, and the todo coupling defers to the plan coupling on it: no prelude or seed, no work nudge or closed-list nudge, and `stop_posture` reads it as quiet, so a stop over running delegated children is the plan coupling's to judge. `loop_coupling.rs::reinjection_text` keeps `frontier_text`, since the todo header carries counts and one running label, not the ready todos with their checks. The plan tool description's "the todo tool is the list" becomes "the todo list shows it", and doctrine's todo, planning and flow passages say an open plan is the list and drop "your todo blocked `on child`"; the request budget falls 47754 to 47688 bytes. The `plan-fanout` surface scenario gains `noRefusalsFrom: ["todo"]`. The D224 row names `dispatch_ready_in`, the probe backstop's name since 0.288.0. The G2 review's findings are folded in, each with a test seen red first: `Run.settle` gives a finished child whose todo the engine left running one wait round and then collects it, and `Run.launch` does not adopt that attempt again, so a run with no budget returns `unresolved` instead of waiting forever; the finish hook runs an uncontracted delegation's accept command (the child's `check`) before the engine's `done` and routes the discoveries of a `{value, discoveries}` answer as `rlm.result` does, and a red check fails the todo with `its check is red: <evidence>`; `done.rs::admit` reads the engine carve-out off `Txn::engine`, set from `Actor::Engine`, so a child named `engine` cannot submit for another child's todo; the start-refusal cache keys a contract refusal on which criteria are stored, so storing a missing checker lets the next op start the todo; a finish the engine could not accept re-reads the todo, says `plan: "alpha" waits on you: <note>` when the verdict blocked it on the user and nothing when an owner op won the race; the hook reads only the root a `<plan>/<todo>` child names and returns at once for any other name. Tests: `todo_mirror::a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids`, `a_mirrored_item_refuses_start_with_the_plan_road`, `a_mirrored_list_with_a_running_child_does_not_nag` (T0) and `a_plan_opened_through_the_tool_is_the_sessions_todo_list` (T1, wiring and the environment's header line); `plan_ops::a_child_named_engine_is_not_the_engine`, `contracts::a_start_refused_for_a_missing_criterion_is_tried_once_it_is_stored`, `contracts::an_escalated_finish_tells_the_owner_the_todo_waits_on_them` and `plan::dispatch::a_red_accept_command_fails_the_finish` (T0, new, the G2 findings); `test_yi_plan::test_a_verdict_that_judges_no_product_leaves_the_attempt_alone` runs without a budget under a five-second bound. Existing todo tests are unchanged. Not built: the `locate` read-cost fix has no test, since its contract is cost; the TUI HUD still shows the plan line above the mirrored list; the `plan-fanout` confirmation, which is paid and user-run. Review fix (Refs #486): the todo tool's description says the open plan is the list, and the plan schema's todos line says `isolation worktree requires a contract`; the request prefix goes from 47669 to 47580 bytes by trimming the todo `op` line and the plan description's ledger sentence (`prompt_drift::the_tool_text_says_the_plan_is_the_list_and_a_worktree_needs_a_contract`). growth +4312: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +252 (the stack of twenty-four stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D226, stands at +16525 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: doctrine's blocked-on-child line, four restating comments; weighed and kept: `frontier_text` in the re-injection. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.288.0 | 2026-09-22 | A plan child's finish is its submission and its acceptance: no owner op is needed to submit, verify, accept, refuse or fail a delegated todo whose child ended (D225, extends D222; Closes #485). This is stage G2 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. `SubagentHost` gains a late-bound `FinishFn` (`set_finished`, the `lease.rs` pattern, since the host is built before the engine), installed by `wire_plan_engine` at depth 0 and holding host and engine weakly; `conclude` consults it after `publish` and the bill for a completed child that is neither asking (D165) nor a service and for a failed or interrupted one, and a child it takes sends no lifecycle notice. The new `plan/finish.rs::finish_child` finds the todo `Running` by that child in an active plan (none means an `rlm.run` child, and conclude's own notice runs), stores the child's last answer in the plan's artifact store as `plan://<id>/artifacts/<digest>` (`application/json` when a declared schema or a schema item reads it), applies `submit` as `Actor::Engine` unless the child submitted for this attempt itself (the phase for a worktree todo, the submitted url for an inline one), then `done`, which for a worktree todo accepts the verified integration and reaps the child, so the promoted transcript and the `history://` pin still reach the owner. A `fail` verdict applies `fail` with cause `contract refused: <items>` and disposition `retained`; an abstention, an escalation or a merge conflict leaves the todo running for the owner's `fail`, `retry` or the user's acceptance; a failed or interrupted child's todo is failed with its error. The owner is told once over the `plan_relevance` Steer that wakes an idle owner: `plan: accepted "alpha" (agent://...)`, `plan: refused "alpha": <item>: <detail>` or `plan: failed "alpha": <cause>`. `done.rs::admit` lets the engine submit for the running child. The child brief loses both submit lines, doctrine's plan flow says the engine starts, submits and accepts or refuses (the request budget stays at 47754 bytes), and `help(yi)`'s Todo line says the engine steps a delegated todo. In the library `Run.settle` reads a delegated todo's state and sends no `done` (a finished child whose todo is still running is the engine's or waits on the owner), `Run._complete` is the inline road alone, and `Todo.cancel` fails a running delegated todo without an interrupt, since the interrupt handed its end to the engine first; the fake host runs the same finish at the wait that reports the child. The G1 review's findings are folded in: the engine's start-refusal cache keeps only a contract refusal, keyed by the contract digest, and a fuse refusal, keyed by the spawn count, so a transient refusal (a full host, a spawn that failed) is tried again by the next op; an engine start another pass got to first (`illegal_step`, `admission`, `unmet_edge`, `needs_reconciliation`) journals and says nothing; the probe backstop, `dispatch_ready_in`, walks only the roots this session's own ledger names; `view` returns the held todos and the engine's start refusal on the current attempt, `plan.op` replies carry `held`, and `Run.launch` answers `admission` only for a held todo and otherwise surfaces the engine's refusal as `not_started`; the review pod holds its declared arbiter on a reader instead of the user; the in-order adopt loop's missing refusal guard is moot, since the settle no longer sends `done` for a delegated todo, and the guard would strand a todo the engine starts after a refusal. Not built: `stale_reminder` stays, since a ready inline todo can still go unclaimed and the reminder counts it; the plan's re-drive of a finished but unsubmitted child after a crash is the existing repair road. Tests: `plan::dispatch::an_inline_childs_finish_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `an_asking_child_is_not_submitted`, `an_rlm_run_child_is_not_a_plan_finish`, `a_brief_never_names_submit` (T0, new) and `a_failed_childs_last_product_survives_the_reap` (rewritten: the engine fails the todo from the child's error); `lanes::accept::a_finished_worktree_child_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `an_early_submit_is_accepted_at_the_finish_and_not_submitted_again` and `a_red_contract_fails_the_todo_retained` (T1, new); `plan_ops::a_transient_spawn_failure_is_tried_again_by_the_next_op`, `a_raced_engine_start_journals_nothing` and `a_view_names_the_todos_the_width_holds` and `plan_probe::the_backstop_starts_only_the_roots_this_session_owns` (T0, new, the G1 findings); `test_yi_plan::test_a_red_verdict_fails_the_attempt_through_the_engine_and_a_stuck_child_is_waited_on` (moved to the engine path), `test_an_engine_refusal_is_not_read_as_a_wait_for_a_slot` (new), the abstain and cancel tests and `test_yi_shapes`' pod and overhead counts (fork_join 9 requests over 2 by hand, scatter 16 over 2) rewritten to pin the engine's finish; `a_worktree_brief_names_the_submit_verb` is deleted with the lines it pinned; the `kernel_data_surface` T2 rigs hand each child's end to `finish_child` at their wait, as the host's hook does, and the fork_join journey pins starts `a, b, c`. Not built: the `plan-child-submit` and `plan-fanout` confirmations the plan names, which are paid and user-run. Review fix (Refs #485): `schedule.rs::done_submitted` runs before the starts of every engine pass and sends `done` with the stored product for a running todo whose `submit` the engine journaled on this attempt and whose child `Liveness` says is gone, once per attempt in a process; `recovery::reconcile` names `submitted` (`plan_recovery::a_submit_the_engine_committed_is_done_by_the_next_pass`); `finish.rs::left_notice` returns a line on every refusal and the hook says the no-todo line when `finish_child` finds none, so the owner always gets exactly one line. Journey fix (Refs #485): `kernel_data_surface::resume_after_a_kernel_death_reuses_results_and_replays_no_cell` found one cell in `program.py`, not two, because the resumed cell's only effect was the child's finish, which the engine now completes; the recording rule stands, and the second cell now declares `package`, an inline todo after `tests`, so its `append` is the cell's own first effect, the journey asserts the cell's `program` record precedes it, and the mark file and spawn count still pin that nothing is replayed or respawned. growth +4060: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +565 (the stack of twenty-three stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D225, stands at +16273 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: the brief's submit lines, `Run._complete`'s child branch and `Todo.cancel`'s interrupt; weighed and kept: `stale_reminder`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |

Tests: `tools::a_write_result_carries_the_tag_an_edit_must_cite`, `a_placeholder_tag_refusal_names_where_the_tag_comes_from`, `an_edit_carries_the_seen_lines_it_did_not_move`, `bash_runs_process_substitution`, `a_wrong_typed_argument_is_not_reported_as_missing`, `todo_e2e::a_label_the_renderer_cut_names_its_item_back`, `an_op_passed_as_a_key_lands_as_that_op`, `plan::dispatch::a_fenced_json_answer_validates_against_a_schema`, `a_worktree_brief_names_the_submit_verb`, `plan_ops::refusals` (five), `plan_ops::width_is_the_family_cap_not_the_host`. | 0.282.0 | 2026-09-21 | A lever moves only through a paired comparison that reports its interval (part of D220, no new decision row; Closes #466). This is stage F4d of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 0 R12, 10.4 and 11), all in `evals/levers.py`, which a person runs and which starts no model. `compare --lever <name> --value <n>` pairs baseline and candidate by repetition, interleaved so provider drift lands on both sides, and reports for tokens, cost, wall and reward an interval for the median paired difference per task: the sign test inverted, an order-statistic interval that assumes only independent pairs, printed with the confidence its pair count really supports (fewer than six pairs cannot reach 0.95, and the number says so). A candidate is `better` only when both gates of 0.281.0 pass and a whole efficiency interval lies below zero at 0.95, `rejected` with its class below a floor, else `inconclusive`; a median alone is never a win. `grid --knob <name>=v1,v2` takes one to five knobs, drops the point that is all defaults, and is refused before anything runs when `(points + 1) * k` passes `--max-runs` (40 by default) or a task has no floor; it reports `spend` as runs and trials, judges each point on its own pairs and keeps the nondominated among those that passed. Each point also reports the median paired difference per task beside the pooled interval, and a grid whose repetitions and tasks cannot make six pairs says so on stderr before it spends anything. A trial row whose metric is a string, a boolean, a NaN or an infinity is refused rather than compared (a NaN reward passes a floor by not being a number), and a candidate that did not run a task the baseline ran is `task_not_run:<task>`, never a saving. An unknown lever, one marked not tunable (its `why` is printed), a non-integer or an out-of-range value is refused before the runner is called, by the rules `Levers::load` applies to the same file. The runner is the owner's command, `<runner> <overrides.json> <task>...` through an argv list, with `YI_LEVERS` naming the same file in the child's environment and this process's own untouched; `--group validation` is selection and the result says which group ran. No least-squares fit over the manifest and no Gaussian process; a later optimizer is its own proposal. Tests on a synthetic runner: `a_comparison_reports_its_interval` (nine pairs, the interleaved order, a two-pair saving that stays inconclusive, a noisy one, a failing one), `a_grid_refuses_a_knob_marked_not_tunable` (and the run bound, the knob bound, spend counted), `a_candidate_outside_its_range_is_refused`. Limits: the pooled interval treats pairs from one task as independent of each other, which is why the per-task medians ride beside it, and it is over the runs that were made, not over unseen tasks; the interval is for the median, so a saving on a minority of tasks reads inconclusive; the final group is not reachable from the command line. | 0.280.0 | 2026-09-21 | The procedural graph is edited offline, under a held-out gate that remembers what it rejected (part of D219, no new decision row; Closes #464). This is stage F4b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 9.4 to 9.8, 10.4 and 11). `evals/graph/refine.py` is stdlib Python a person runs; nothing in the runtime calls it and it starts no model: the scoring run is the owner's `--runner` command (`<runner> <graph.json> <task>...`, one JSON row per trial in the shape `evals/axes.py` scores). Proposals are data: a JSONL of `add_edge`, `drop_edge` and `reword` with the full edge and a rationale, at most 20 a run, an edge addressed by `(from, relation, to, condition)`. `read_proposals` refuses the whole file when a line names a validation or final task of `evals/levers/split.json`: the line is decoded and matched by token, so an id written as a JSON escape is still the id and an id inside a longer word is not a reference; a development task may be named. Per edit, in order and fail closed: the rejection memory (the same `editHash` under the same `baseVersion`, `protocol` and `model` is skipped before anything runs; a rejection under another configuration counts in `earlierRejections` and the edit runs), `apply` (`malformed_edit`, `edge_exists`, `no_such_edge`, `no_change`), then `check`, which is `Graph::check` rule for rule plus `parse` for what serde refuses, so an invalid edit is rejected under the rule's name and never reaches a run. A survivor is scored on the development tasks (`fit_passes_dropped`), and only then once on the validation tasks: promoted when the held-out pass count is not lower (ties accepted) and tokens per solved task (`input + cacheRead + output` over every trial, divided by tasks solved; section 9.8) are not higher, else `held_out_passes_dropped` or `tokens_per_solved_rose`; when neither graph solves anything there is no price per solved task, so the byte rule stands alone and an edit that grew the rendered bytes is `guidance_bytes_unpaid`. That is the efficiency gate: a line that grows the rendered bytes rides every request that carries it, so it is promoted only when the tasks solved pay for it, and each verdict records the edit's `guidanceBytes` delta. A promoted edit is the next base at `version + 1`. Rejections are appended to `evals/fixtures/graph/rejected.jsonl` as `{editHash, baseVersion, protocol, model, reason, scores, at}`; the hash covers the kind and the edge, not the rationale. `--write` writes the graph in `dump`'s form, one node and one edge per line, and `graph.json` was rewritten into it (the same JSON value; F4a's byte-identical test passes). The two rule sets cannot drift quietly: `evals/fixtures/graph/structural.json` holds the closed condition list and 21 graphs, valid at every limit and invalid one rule at a time, and `graph::the_shared_fixture_is_judged_alike_on_both_sides` (yi-types) and the Python test of the same name both judge it; moving `MAX_GUIDANCE_BYTES` on one side was watched red. `split.json`, shared with F4c, draws development and validation over the seven synthetic tasks of `evals/fixtures/tasks`, final empty (section 10.4). Tests (stdlib unittest, `evals/graph/test_refine.py`, run by `evals/selftest.py` as `check_graph_refiner`): `a_graph_edit_that_drops_the_held_out_score_is_rejected_and_remembered`, `a_proposal_naming_a_held_out_task_is_refused`, `a_structurally_invalid_edit_never_reaches_a_run`, `ties_are_accepted_and_tokens_per_solved_task_may_not_rise` and the fixture test, all on a synthetic runner, each gate watched red with its line removed. Beside it, every fact a seam asserts is now spelled once in `yi_types::graph` (`RESULT_OK`, `RESULT_ERROR`, `GRID_ANSWER_EMPTY`, `COROUTINE_UNAWAITED`, `METHOD_AWAITED`, `LISTING_NAME_MISSED`, `SESSION_ON_DISK`, `SESSION_IN_MEMORY`, `CHILD_RUNNING`, `CHILD_FINISHED`, `CHILD_STATE`, `TODO_STATE`), and `PREDICATES` is built from the same constants, so a mistyped fact cannot compile into a line that silently stops firing; `graph::every_fact_a_seam_asserts_parses` covers the two the seams compose an argument onto. Behaviour an existing caller can see: none. Limits: held-out detection is a token match on the decoded line, which can still refuse an innocent proposal whose prose uses a task id as an ordinary word; the refiner trusts the runner's rows; a candidate takes effect only in a binary rebuilt with it, which is the runner's business. Not run: any real scoring, which is paid and the owner's. growth +2745: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this stage is +18 (the stack of sixteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D219, stands at +14958 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): the review fix that gives every fact a seam asserts one spelling in `yi_types::graph`, the `evals/` Python (refiner and tests) being outside the measure. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.279.0 | 2026-09-21 | The host's next-step lines are a procedural graph the binary carries, and the string builders that wrote them are gone (D219; Closes #463). This is stage F4a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 9.1 to 9.4, 9.7, 11 and 12). `crates/types/src/graph.rs` holds `Graph { version, nodes, edges }`: a node names one of Yi's verbs (`tool`, `op` or `request`), an edge is `(from, relation, to)` with a `condition`, `guidance`, `pitfalls` and a `weight`, and the relations are `then`, `instead`, `before`, `after_error` and `after_refusal`. A condition is a `Predicate`, a string that parses only when it is a name from the closed table `PREDICATES` with one of the arguments that name takes (`always`, `result_ok`, `result_error(<kind>)`, `output_capped`, `todo_open`, `todo_state(running|pending|blocked)`, `plan_ready_nonempty`, `child_state(<state>)`, `blocked_on(user|child|external)`, `worktree_unmerged`, `done_refused`, `inbox_nonempty`, and the six states the old producers branched on: `coroutine_unawaited`, `method_awaited`, `listing_name_missed`, `grid_answer_empty`, `session_on_disk`, `session_in_memory`); there is no expression language, unknown fields are refused, and no model evaluates a condition: the host asserts the predicates that hold as facts at the seam and the renderer compares names. `Graph::check(verbs)` holds the structural rules of section 9.4 in a fixed order and returns the name of the one that failed (`unregistered_node`, `too_many_edges`, `unknown_node`, `self_edge`, `too_many_out_edges`, `guidance_size`, `pitfalls_size`, `unreachable`), which is what the offline refiner of F4b records. `crates/runtime/src/prompts/graph.json` is compiled in with `include_str!` and parsed once behind a `OnceLock` (`affordance::shipped`): no file read at run time, no environment variable, no reload; a graph that failed to parse would render nothing, and the structural test is what keeps that from shipping. `affordance::render(graph, last_call, facts)` localizes by exact match on the last call, walks two hops of edges whose conditions hold, sorts by weight, substitutes the one `{name}` a line cites, deduplicates by text and truncates at `facts.cap`: `TOOL_LINES` (2) for a tool result, and the todo tool keeps `NEXT_LINES` (3) in `next_lines`, which renders one item at a time. `affordance::next(last_call, holds, name)` is the joined form the spawn reply, the child notice and the compaction notice use. The migration is byte-identical: `affordance::every_line_rendered_today_renders_from_the_graph` pinned every line of `spawned`, `coroutine_leak`, `method_awaited`, `listing_name`, `child_finished`, `grid_empty`, both branches of `compacted`, `call_template` and the todo tool's `moves` (ids and quoted labels, the cap of three, the blocked fallback, a name that contains `{name}`) as golden bytes against the old producers in its own commit, and the same goldens pass from the graph. With that the seven string builders in `affordance.rs` and `todo/text.rs::moves`'s match were dead and are deleted; `NEXT`, `append` and `call_template` (derived from a schema, never a fixed string) stay, and `moves` is now four lines over the renderer. `tools.rs` replaces `grid_note` and `ipython_note` with `facts_of`, which asserts `result_ok` or `result_error(<errorKind>)`, `grid_answer_empty` and at most one ipython needle, in the order the old code tried them, so one result still carries one line. The spawn reply localizes at `rlm.run` for `rlm.service` too, because `service::handle` is one function behind one admission. Tests (T0): `affordance::the_shipped_graph_passes_every_structural_check` checks the shipped graph against the builtin tool names, `ipython`, `plan`, `todo`, the todo ops and every host request read from the runtime's own `register("…")` calls; `affordance::an_unknown_last_call_renders_nothing`; `affordance::rendering_stops_at_two_hops_and_three_lines`; `graph::an_unknown_predicate_fails_to_parse` parses every name and argument in the table and refuses eleven near misses; every earlier affordance string test is kept, pointed at the renderer. Behaviour an existing caller can see: none; `tool_surface.json`, `request_budget.json` and the behaviour cassettes are unchanged, and `schemas.lock` gains the six `graph::` shapes. The kernel-dead path: the graph is host data and renders for the JSON tools the same. Limits: the facts `output_capped`, `todo_open`, `plan_ready_nonempty`, `blocked_on`, `worktree_unmerged`, `done_refused` and `inbox_nonempty` are vocabulary no seam asserts yet, so an edge on one never fires until a later stage asserts it; the todo producer localizes at the tool, not the op, because its lines are the same after every op; plan ops are not nodes until an edge needs one. Not built: the value comparison of the strings, a plain rule table and the graph on the same tasks (section 9.1), which is a paid measurement and the owner's; until its row exists the graph has earned only equivalence. growth +2727: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this stage is +207 against 270 estimated (the stack of fifteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D219, stands at +14940 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 180 lines in yi-types against 90, of which the predicate table is 22 and `check` 46, and 27 net in yi-runtime against 180 (121 added, 94 deleted), because the deleted builders paid for the renderer; the procedural graph and its renderer, and the affordance strings became data. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.278.0 | 2026-09-21 | A service is a child with a stable address: its name is reserved, a crashed run is respawned under it with the transcript and inbox kept, within a restart intensity and on a freshly drawn lease (D218, extends D165, D214, D215 and D216; Closes #462). This is stage F3c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 4.2, 7.1, 7.3 and 11). `rlm.service(name, brief, restart=3, **kwargs)` sends the new host request `rlm.service`; `SubagentHost::service` (`crates/runtime/src/subagent/service.rs`) refuses an empty name, `parent`, `all` and `isolation="worktree"` (a respawn has no lane to settle the last incarnation's checkout into), attaches when a live service holds the name with the same brief (`attached: true`, the same child id), refuses another brief, and otherwise admits through `admit`, the body `spawn` and `spawn_seated` already shared. An ordinary `rlm.run` asking for a service's name meets the spawn's own `already taken` refusal, which holds through a respawn because the record never leaves the roster. The respawn is `SubagentHost::respawn`, called from `conclude` (the tail of `run_child`, split out so a woken turn can reach it) and deciding under the roster lock: only `Failed { Provider }` and `Failed { KernelDeath }` respawn, never `Completed`, `Interrupted`, `Reaped`, `Repossessed`, a red check, a refused spawn or a deadline; never a service `revoke` or the parent's `close` marked stopped; and never past `restart` respawns inside `RESTART_WINDOW_MS` (600,000), which `MAX_RESTARTS` caps at ten so a payload cannot ask for a crash loop (refused, never clamped). It returns the dead incarnation's lease (`return_lease`, journaled as F2b journals every return), draws the next with the kept kwargs through `draw`, so an ask the parent can no longer cover is refused with both numbers and never clamped, builds the session outside the lock (`build.rs`: `cast` and `build`, moved out of `spawn_seated`), attaches it to the same session store, reads the stopped mark again under the lock before installing it (the build is the one stretch a respawn holds no lock across, so a `revoke` or a `close` that lands there ends the service rather than being overruled, and the built session's kernel is disposed, as the dead incarnation's is), and steps the record with `Step::Respawn`: exit cleared, phase `Queued`, the counters reset and `billed_from` set to the kept transcript's length, which `refold` and the lease return both count from, so a lagged watch cannot charge a predecessor's turns to the successor's lease (`recursion_e2e::a_respawned_service_is_billed_from_its_own_first_turn`). No terminal update is published for a run that was respawned, so F1e's one ending per record holds. A refused respawn leaves the record `Failed` with `<cause>; not respawned: <why>` as its error and its notice (`recursion_e2e::a_service_out_of_restarts_or_lease_ends_failed_and_says_so`). A service's later turns have no `run_child` behind them, so `watch` hands `AgentEnd` to `turn_ended`, which reads the settled turn's exit for a service whose first run completed and sends a crash down the same road, one reader at a time (mail can start a turn on a run that already crashed, and two readers of that one crash would end it twice) (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_crash_respawns_and_a_parent_close_ends_a_service_for_good`). `ChildRecord.juror` is now `standing: Standing { Worker, Juror, Service(Service) }`. `reserve` counts only workers against `max_children` and caps only workers, so a service never reaped does not pin a slot and eight retained workers do not refuse one; the depth exemption stays a juror's alone, a service takes no `Purpose::Verification` seat (it has no seat argument), and the family cap, the lease and the wall bind it like any child (`recursion_e2e::a_service_is_outside_the_worker_cap_and_under_the_depth_limit`). An idle service's `Completed` exit sends no lifecycle notice, since there is nothing to reap; a question it ended on still reaches the parent. `yi_types::mail::Envelope` gains `fromIncarnation` and `toIncarnation`, both `Option<u32>` and omitted when absent, so an inbox or a fixture written before this stage parses and re-serializes byte for byte (`mail::an_envelope_round_trips_in_the_wire_spelling_the_inbox_stores`); `schemas.lock` moved for `mail::Envelope` alone. The host fills both from the registry at `Desk::seal`: a service's incarnation, `None` for the parent, an ordinary child and an address nobody live holds. `present` shows `from_incarnation="n"` and `to_incarnation="n"` on a message a service sent or was addressed, so the incarnation that reads a kept transcript can tell its predecessor's conversation from its own. A kept inbox therefore says which incarnation each message was addressed to, the respawn's brief tells the new incarnation that mail addressed to an earlier one was its predecessor's conversation, the dead session's queues die with it so nothing is presented to the successor on its own, and a request parked on the dead incarnation is refused at once by name (`"index" was respawned before it replied to <id>`). `status()` members carry `service` and `incarnation`. Test: `recursion_e2e::a_service_respawns_under_its_name_and_keeps_its_inbox` (T1) holds incarnation 1 open on a tool call, sends it mail and a request, asks for the name through `rlm.run`, attaches through the registered `rlm.service` request, lets the run crash, and reads the refusal of the parked request, one record, both incarnations' stamps in one inbox, `history://index` with both briefs, and no notice; every control above was watched red with its line removed. Behaviour an existing caller can see: `rlm.status()` members gain two keys; a worker spawn's validation order is unchanged; `tool_surface.json` is unchanged (the kernel surface is not a tool); no prompt fragment moved. The kernel-dead path: a service is a kernel call, and with no kernel there is no `rlm` at all; mail to a name and `history://<name>` keep working from the JSON tools. Limits: restart counts and incarnations live in the host and restart with it, as its children do; the respawned session loads its predecessor's transcript, so a crash caused by that context recurs until compaction or the intensity ends it; a turn that reported no usage (every provider error) leaves that incarnation's returned lease wholly spent, F2b's rule; a service cannot hold a worktree; and the waiter carries no incarnation of its own, because the respawn retires every waiter on the name first. Not built: a health `cmd` and a shutdown contract for a service (section 6.2's table row), and adoption of a service by a restarted host. growth +2520: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this stage is +430 (the stack of fourteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D218, stands at +14733 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 424 lines in yi-runtime against 140 estimated and 6 in yi-types, with 24 of Python against 40. The overshoot is the woken-turn road (a service lives in turns no `run_child` watches, which the estimate did not see), the lease return and redraw under the roster lock, attach-or-refuse, and the `rlm.service` registration; weighed for deletion and taken: the respawn reuses the record instead of retiring and re-admitting it (no reserved-name set, no gap to guard, no second terminal update), `cast` and `build` moved to `build.rs` and are shared by both roads so `subagent.rs` shrank by 6 lines, the spawn reply is one `handle` for both requests, an ordinary child holding the name falls through to the existing refusal, `revoke` marks the service stopped so one guard covers both deliberate stops, and `status()` reads the incarnation through one accessor without widening `MemberView`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: a respawn moves the steers and follow-ups the dead run never drained onto the new session before it disposes the old one, so a send whose receipt said `queued` or `woken` still reaches a turn. Two claims corrected: nothing constructs `Failed { KernelDeath }` yet, so only `Failed { Provider }` respawns today; and the expire-versus-conclude double ending is pinned by `recursion_e2e::a_child_that_ends_while_expire_joins_a_sibling_keeps_its_one_ending`, while the `turn_ended` reader bit still has no harness of its own (Refs #462). Test: `recursion_e2e::a_service_respawns_under_its_name_and_keeps_its_inbox` now asserts the queued body reaches incarnation 2. |
| 0.277.0 | 2026-09-21 | A review pod is a recipe: readers with distinct briefs whose findings are evidence, and one arbiter whose command is the verdict (D217, extends D212 and D216; Closes #461). This is stage F3b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8.4, 8.5 and 11). `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/recipes/review_pod.py` is the first file under `yi/recipes/`. `declare(plan, subject, check, arbiter=None)` declares one `Reader` per brief in `BRIEFS` (correctness, tests, scope), each bound to `subject` with its brief as the role's note and answering `shapes.ANSWER`, and an `arbiter` todo after them, a worktree `Writer` unless another role is named, whose contract is `check`. `review_pod(plan, run)` is module-level like the shapes, so the run lease's identity check attaches a second `plan.run(shape=review_pod)` to the first. It is no third scheduler: the readers run under `shapes._schedule` with its one_for_one restart, each answer goes through `shapes._survivor` (a failed reader is retried and dropped so the plan can finish, a null answer abstains, and a finding survives only with a quote `verify_quotes` found on the cited line inside that reader's own partition), and the arbiter is issued by `shapes._asked`, which gained a `context` argument. No plan op rewrites a declared todo's note, so the arbiter runs as `<key>-r2`, the way scatter issues a reader again per round: the issue copies the declared delegation and contract, carries the surviving findings in `delegation.note` cut to the host's 1 KiB `InlineNote` cap (on a whole byte sequence, and the cut says it happened, because half a finding reads as a different finding), and carries every reader's whole answer as `context` urls; the declared arbiter is then dropped. The issue is started once under `_schedule` with no restart, so it is verified once: a red command fails the pod and nothing asks again. Geometry is checked before the first start and names every problem at once: fewer than two readers, a reader with no note or with another reader's note, a reader with no `schema` item, anything but exactly one todo that is not a reader, an arbiter with no delegate, an arbiter with no critical `cmd` or `example` item, and an arbiter carrying a `judge` item (a judged arbiter meets no floor, and one verdict would spend the todo's juries). Tests: `test_yi_shapes::test_a_pod_verdict_is_the_arbiters_command_not_a_reader` (T0: two blockers and a green command pass, three clean readers and a red command fail, an invented quote never reaches the note, one start and no retry), `test_yi_shapes::test_a_pod_without_a_code_arbiter_or_distinct_briefs_is_refused` (T0), and the journey `kernel_data_surface::review_pod_on_the_fixture_repo` (T2: the recipe in a real kernel over `fixtures/plans/worktree/repo.sh`, the real step table and the real `cmd` verifier; the journey's rig moved into a `crewed` helper the F1b journey now shares). Behaviour an existing caller can see: `yi.review_pod` is a new public name with a docstring example, and `help(yi)` lists it; nothing else moved, no prompt fragment changed and `tool_surface.json` is unchanged. | 0.276.0 | 2026-09-20 | A judged contract item is a walled reader of another model family answering a fixed schema, its quotes checked against its fetch log, aggregated in Rust (D216, extends D194 and D215; Closes #460).
