The `judge` decider has been on the wire since F0c and refused at declaration; `Contract::validate` now accepts it with a jury of one or three (`ContractError::JurySize` for any other size, replacing `JudgeUnavailable`) and still refuses one that stands alone, because a judge item is neither behavioural nor shape and meets no floor (`contract::validate_enforces_the_floors_and_a_judge_never_stands_alone`). The jury is `crates/runtime/src/plan/judge.rs`, one more decider bound into `Verifier::run` through the `Judge` trait F0c left open; there is no second verification path. `Jury::judge` seats `policy.n` children through `SubagentHost::spawn_seated`, each on a model of another family than the owner's. The family is the vendor segment of a registry model (`subagent/models.rs`: `family_of`, `other_families`, with `find_models` moved beside them): `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` is `z-ai` and `openrouter/anthropic/...` is `anthropic`. The owners are the host's own model, read from its settings, and the selector the plan spawned the todo's child with, resolved in the registry; nothing a payload says names a family, and with no other family on offer the item abstains `no other family` and nothing is spawned (`judge::a_judge_is_another_family_or_the_item_abstains`). The candidates are the registry's models on the host's own provider or on a provider whose key is set, cheapest input first. The brief (`prompts/judge.md`, a user message to the juror and no part of any cached prefix) is the rubric, the evidence addresses and the schema; `brief` takes nothing else, the spawn carries no `fork` and no `context`, and the evidence is served through the juror's own `fetch`, never inlined (`judge::the_brief_carries_no_history_or_prior_verdict`). The juror's wall denies every write under the workspace and every URL scheme but `local://`; it is cooperative, as every wall is. The data rule is `auto_review.md`'s, word for word. The answer is `yi_types::plan::contract::JurorAnswer`, parsed with `deny_unknown_fields`: an empty answer, a bare word, fenced JSON, trailing prose, a missing or unknown key and a fourth verdict word each abstain, and so does a decided vote that quotes nothing (`judge::malformed_or_empty_answers_abstain`). Every quote must name an evidence address, the juror's own transcript must hold a whole read of it at the frozen hash (`fetch::rows_of`, `fetch::as_served`), and the quoted line must be that line, which a blank one never is; any miss abstains the whole item, above the tally, and marks the dropped juror's line with the check's own `unbacked` flag rather than a prefix of the reason the juror wrote (`judge::an_unbacked_quote_abstains_the_item`, the `quotes` rows of `fixtures/plans/judge/jurors.json`). Evidence that tells its juror to pass and quote nothing therefore produces an abstention from a juror that obeys and a backed fail from one that does not (`judge::evidence_carrying_instructions_is_data`, the `steered` rows). `tally` computes the quorum over the seats asked for: one judge decides alone, of three two like votes decide, and one pass with two abstentions abstains (`judge::one_pass_and_two_abstentions_abstain`). Each juror's model, vote, clipped reason and `unbacked` flag ride `ItemLine.jurors` into the journal's verdict, and the `done` or `done_refused` record a session sees carries the counts as `jurors`, which `extract.py` reads as `juror_pass`, `juror_fail`, `juror_abstain` and `quotes_dropped`. Bounds: the done path reserves `Purpose::Verification` for a judged contract and hands the permit to the jury as its `Seat`; `spawn_seated` reads the permit's purpose, and a juror neither fills the worker cap nor is refused by it or by the depth limit, while the family cap and the owner's lease (100,000 tokens a juror, returned at retire) still bind (`judge::full_capacity_still_adjudicates_through_the_reservation`). A juror is retired, never reaped, and its ending is not a notice to the owner it judged. `juries` counts the `verification_requested` records on the todo at the plan's version; past `JUDGE_CAP_PER_TODO` (3) `Verifier::run` escalates the item without seating anyone, and `refuse` blocks a todo on the user at once on an `Escalate` outcome, under a note that names the escalation instead of a refusal tally that never ran (`plan_ops::the_fourth_jury_on_one_todo_escalates_to_the_user`). The whole-verification deadline is read once ahead of every decider rather than inside `run_item`, so no jury is seated for an item with no time left to read it. Handed down by F2b and built: the engine's spawn road computes the child's effective wall (`SubagentHost::wall_for`, which `spawn` now shares) and refuses a delegation whose `context` names a URL that wall denies, the parent's own wall included (`judge::a_brief_naming_context_its_wall_denies_is_refused`). Behaviour an existing caller can see: a contract with a `judge` item is declarable; an `Escalate` verdict blocks its todo on the user on the first refusal rather than the third; a delegation whose context its wall denies is refused the spawn where it used to fail one fetch later; `done` and `done_refused` session records may carry `jurors`. No prompt fragment of the working model moved and `tool_surface.json` is unchanged. Limits a deployment reaches before calibration does: a `plans.dir` outside the cwd puts the evidence blobs outside the tree a juror reads, so every judged item abstains there; a host whose own grant is smaller than three juror leases has a seat refused and abstains by quorum; and a todo the plan holds no delegation for contributes no owner family beyond the host's own model, because `spawn_result` records the agent name and not the model a child executed on. Not built, and said in the plan: a jury on the worktree paths (`submit`'s candidate check and the staging check pass no seat, so a judged item abstains there; seating one on each would spend two of a todo's three juries on one submit, which needs a decision first); a `judge(...)` builder in the `yi` library (a judged item is declared through the contract's JSON; section 6.4 puts calibration before default use); a `judge` record kind of its own in `ops.jsonl` (the juror lines ride the verdict the `done` and `done_refused` records already carry) and a `todo.juries` field (the count is derived from the journal); a fragment or paged read as backing for a quote (the hash is the whole file's, so a juror that reads a span is unbacked and abstains); a configured judge model (cheapest other family is the reader rule); a `kernel dead` abstention of its own (a juror that cannot fetch cannot quote, and abstains by the quote rule); and the plan journal's `Disposition::RepossessionPending`, which still needs a road from `SubagentHost` into the engine's journal: this stage opened the opposite road, from the engine's verifier into the host, and the repossession happens on the probe loop's timer where no engine transaction is open. growth +2090: three stages measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this one is +596 (the stack of twelve stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D216, stands at +14303 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 549 lines in yi-runtime against 320 estimated and 47 in yi-types, with 7 of Python. The overshoot is the seat (the verification permit carried from the done path through `Snapshot` into `spawn_seated`, which the estimate took for granted), the jury count and the escalation arm in `done.rs`, the juror lines and their session-visible counts, and the context refusal handed down by F2b; weighed for deletion and taken: the tests live in `tests/judge.rs` and not in `src`, `find_models` moved out of `subagent.rs` so that file shrank by 6 lines, `relevance_of` now reads through `rows_of` and does not share a copy of the loop, the juror schema is a serde type in yi-types and no hand parser, the jury cap sits in the verifier so the engine test needs no host, and `spawn` and the delegate share one `wall_for`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.275.0 | 2026-09-20 | A child holds a lease drawn from its parent; revoke has a grace and a visible repossession record; walls shrink hereditarily and holds compile down at spawn; and a child ends on one typed exit every surface reads the same way (D215, extends D165, D210 and D214; Closes #459). This is stage F2b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 7.4 to 7.6 and 11) with the child lifecycle audit's items a to e. The exit: `yi_types::subagent::ChildExit` (`Completed`, `Failed { class }`, `Interrupted`, `Reaped`, `Repossessed`) replaces F1e's forced status; `family::read_exit` is the one function that turns it into the wire `ChildStatus`, the `MemberState` and the notice's verb (`subagent::status_state_and_notice_derive_from_one_exit`). `ChildStatus` keeps its three words and `ChildUpdate` gains an optional `exit`, so an older client reads what it read. `ChildRecord::step` in the new `crates/runtime/src/subagent/record.rs` is the one writer of a record's lifecycle fields; the watch, `run_child`, `deliver_to_parent`, the lane restore and `retire` call it. A member admitted and not yet polled reads `queued`, which `yi.plan`'s scheduler waits on (`test_yi_plan.test_a_queued_child_is_waited_on`). `stuck` is read from a typed `LoopSignal` the loop and the todo coupling write beside their records, never from a record's name (`family::stuck_reads_the_typed_signal`). The TUI's stop and ACP's `_yi/child_abort` go through `SubagentHost::interrupt`, and `ChildView.session` is a `ChildFeed` that can subscribe and read and cannot abort (`recursion_e2e::a_tui_stop_is_a_host_interrupt`). The lease: `crates/types/src/lease.rs` is the wire (`Lease`, `Revocation`, `Repossession`, `Returned`, `LeaseRecord`, `ParentClose`) and `crates/runtime/src/lease.rs` the mechanism. `rlm.run` takes `deadline_s`, `tokens` and `parent_close`, and the engine's dispatch draws `SpawnSpec.budget` as its child's `tokens`, so both spawn roads are leased (`plan::dispatch::kwargs_draw_the_specs_budget_as_the_childs_lease`); `SubagentHost::draw` runs under the roster lock, refuses an ask past the parent's clock less a 30 s cleanup grace or past its unreserved tokens with both numbers, never clamps, and gives an omitted deadline the inherited bound, which becomes the child session's own `deadline:` clock (`subagent::a_deadline_past_the_parents_bound_is_refused_with_both_numbers`, `subagent::a_spawn_asking_past_the_parents_deadline_or_tokens_is_refused_with_both_numbers`). The reservation returns once as the record leaves, journaled with what was spent; a turn that reported no usage returns nothing (`plan_ledger::reap_records_the_unspent_lease`). `rlm.revoke(name, grace_s, reason)` journals the revocation on the parent's transcript as `custom{lease}`, sends a `cancel`, and registers the grace with the probe loop's `wake_at`; a revoked child is admitted no new work. The ladder's lease job runs on every wake before the in-flight-probe check, so a slow probe never delays an expiry (`plan_probe::a_revoke_due_time_wakes_the_loop_past_a_slow_probe`). At expiry the run is stopped and joined, the lane settles as `Retained` so uncommitted work is committed on its branch (`lanes::a_repossessed_worktree_keeps_its_work_on_its_branch`), the `Repossession` is journaled inside `retire_as` before the record is released or the terminal update published, and the parent gets one `failure` (`recursion_e2e::revoke_delivers_cancel_then_repossesses_after_grace_with_the_record_first`). A stop, settle or journal write that fails leaves the member `repossession_pending` with its worktree and record intact, retried on the next wake (a journal write that fails after the lane has already settled is the one loss: the retry's record names the transcript and not the branch, which is kept and reaped as an orphan lane) (`recursion_e2e::a_failed_settle_reports_repossession_pending_with_references_kept`); the first expiry after a restart completes any revocation the journal left open (`recursion_e2e::restart_during_grace_completes_the_repossession`). `SpawnSpec.parent_close` is `terminate` with a grace (default 30 s) or `request_cancel`; `abandon` is refused until a supervisor exists (`subagent::abandon_is_refused_until_a_supervisor_exists`); `SubagentHost::close` applies it and ACP calls it when a session handle drops. `recursion_e2e::children_at_parent_close_today` was written first and pins that a dropped host handle still stops nothing, then the default `close`. A child's wall is its own under its parent's (`Wall::under`, `subagent::a_child_cannot_spawn_with_a_smaller_wall_than_its_parent`); `gate::compile_ask` denies a detached child's `Ask` with the hazard named, and the broker's no-asker arm now reads its refusal from it (`gate::a_detached_childs_ask_compiles_to_deny_and_an_attached_ones_stays_a_question`). From the F2a review: a removed or failed respondent refuses its waiters at once and names how it ended (`recursion_e2e::a_terminated_respondent_refuses_its_waiters_by_name`); a `cancel` starts no turn, sets a flag the loop reads at its message boundary, and the run reads `interrupted` with cause `cancelled` (`recursion_e2e::a_cancel_ends_the_run_at_its_next_message_boundary`); a child's own `failure` is a verdict, not an ending: it reads `failed` in `wait` at once while its run keeps its absent exit, and the run's own end turns it into `Failed { red_check }`, so one run still publishes one terminal update and a revoked child cannot declare itself out of its grace (`recursion_e2e::a_failure_from_a_child_reads_failed_in_wait`, and the dodge inside `revoke_delivers_cancel_then_repossesses_after_grace_with_the_record_first`); the cancel's receipt word comes from `follow_up_message`, never a status read. Behaviour an existing caller can see: `rlm.status` and `rlm.wait` may answer `queued` and `repossession_pending`; a delegation that names a `budget` is refused the spawn when its parent cannot lease it, where the number used to ride the brief as advice; a child deleted while running carries `exit: reaped` beside the unchanged `error: interrupted`; the environment's children line may open with a `queued` group; the prompt fragments are untouched. Not built, and said in the plan: the attached child's `Ask` as a `request` envelope (the family shares one broker, so the question already reaches the root's surface), the refusal of a spawn whose brief names a walled path, token exhaustion ending a run (tokens are reserved and accounted, not enforced mid-run), process-group kill beyond what `abort` already does to bash and the kernel cell, re-adopting an orphan lane after a restart (the record names it; `yi lanes` reaps it), `close` on the CLI's own exit paths, producers for the `kernel_death` class (a child's own `failure` is the one producer of `red_check`), the plan journal's own `Disposition::RepossessionPending` (F3a owns the road from the host into the engine's journal; until then a repossessed worktree todo reaches the engine as a child it cannot vouch for, blocks on the user, and records its disposition through `fail` or `drop`), and a root's own token grant (no flag sets one, so the token refusal binds what a child drew for its grandchildren and never a root's first child). growth +1494: two stages measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this one is +1032 (the stack of eleven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D215, stands at +13707 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 146 lines in yi-types against 130 estimated, 881 in yi-runtime against 440, of which 60 are the review pass that follows this row (the verdict phase, the engine's budget draw, the build reservation and the two tests that die with them), 2 in yi-acp, 3 in yi-loop, 1 in yi-console and yi-tui one line smaller, with 23 of Python against 30. The runtime overshoot is the journal-resume path, the pending state with its retry, the return accounting with unknown usage, the read-only `ChildFeed`, and the waiter refusal, none of which the estimate priced; weighed for deletion and taken: `retire` gained a `commit` hook instead of a second removal path, `settle_or_restore` and the commit refusal share one `restore`, `fold_event`, `preview` and `update` moved out of `subagent.rs` rather than being wrapped, the record's `status`, `queued` and `repossessing` fields collapsed into `exit` and one `Phase`, the lease timer reuses the probe loop's due-time set instead of a task of its own, the TUI's stop lost its direct-abort arm, and the lease tests in four test files share one `tests/support/family.rs` harness. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: `expire` reads its due list once, so a child whose run ended while an earlier one was joined was repossessed on top of its own ending, and its lease was journaled `Repossessed` and then `Returned`; `repossess` now leaves a record that already has an exit alone. `resume_revocations` no longer skips a lease record it cannot read, which may be what closed the lease it would complete again: it refuses the resume and says so in a notice. `ChildExit` and `FailClass` decode a kind this build does not know to `Other`, read as failed with the verb `ended`, so an update from a newer host keeps its status. Nothing constructs `FailClass::KernelDeath` yet: a kernel that dies reaches the child as a tool error (Refs #459). Tests: `recursion_e2e::a_child_that_ends_while_expire_joins_a_sibling_keeps_its_one_ending`, `recursion_e2e::a_lease_record_this_build_cannot_read_refuses_the_resume` and `yi-types lease::an_exit_from_a_newer_host_keeps_the_update_that_carries_it`. |
| 0.274.0 | 2026-09-20 | Messages are envelopes with kinds, receipts, per-pair order and a durable inbox; a request awaits its reply (D214, extends D165; Closes #458). This is stage F2a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 7.1 to 7.3 and 11), with the child lifecycle audit's finding folded in: `rlm.send` without `followup=True` put the message on the follow-up queue of a child whose turn was over, which nothing drains, and the receipt said `queued`. `crates/types/src/mail.rs` is the wire: `Envelope`, `Kind`, `MailId`, `Delivery` and `Receipt`. `crates/runtime/src/mail.rs` holds the host's mail state (`Desk`: the id counter, the per-pair `seq` counters and the request waiters, under one lock held across a routing), the payload parser `Draft::from_payload`, the inbox write and the presented message; `mailbox.rs` keeps the one route and the one delivery and builds on them, and no second mailbox exists beside the first. The host fills the sender, recipient, id and order, and a payload naming them is ignored (`recursion_e2e::the_host_names_the_sender_and_a_kind_keeps_its_direction`). The envelope is appended to the receiver's session store before anything is delivered, for a live, finished or reaped child and for the parent, and is read with `history://<agent>/since/<seq>/custom/agent_message`; `custom/<type>` is a new closing segment of any `history://` listing, it pages as the listing does, a read does not grow it, and `self` names the reader (`recursion_e2e::a_message_to_a_finished_child_is_inboxed_and_readable_by_history`, `fetch_session::a_walk_of_this_sessions_own_history_ends_at_a_limit_of_one`). A receipt is `{target, id, state}` and the state is read off the delivery attempt: `queued`, `woken` or `inboxed` (`recursion_e2e::send_returns_queued_woken_or_inboxed`, `recursion_e2e::a_send_to_an_idle_child_is_woken_or_inboxed_never_queued`); `AgentSession::deliver` now answers whether it started the turn and `follow_up_message` whether one was running to drain it, each under the lock that settles it, so no word is a status read that raced the turn's end; `wake_idle_hook` queues before it spawns so two lifecycle notices to one turn keep their order (`recursion_e2e::two_messages_from_one_sender_drain_in_seq_order`). `agent_message.request` installs its waiter before the envelope can reach the respondent, resolves only on that respondent's `reply` to that id addressed back to the sender that asked, and retires the waiter once on a reply, a timeout, a refused send or a cancelled cell (`recursion_e2e::request_returns_the_matching_reply_and_times_out_without_one`, and live through a kernel in `recursion_e2e::rlm_run_round_trips_through_a_real_kernel`). A body over 16 KiB is refused with the alternative named and nothing trimmed (`recursion_e2e::a_body_over_sixteen_kib_is_refused_not_trimmed`). Python: `rlm.send` keeps its positional call and gains `reply_to`, `kind`, `ref`, `conversation` and `deadline_ms`; `rlm.request`; `yi.mail` with `send`, `request` and `inbox` (`test_yi_mail.py`). Behaviour an existing caller can see: a plain send to a child that is not running answers `inboxed` where it answered `queued`, a followup answers `queued` or `woken` where it answered `delivered`, and every receipt carries an `id`; the prompt fragments are untouched. Not built, and named in the plan: incarnations on the envelope (nothing respawns a name before F3c), the cancel flag in the child's loop and `failed` from a `failure` envelope (F2b), `progress` into `status().note`, progress coalescing, a bound on inbox growth, and the list of outstanding conversations after a restart; a message to the parent keeps the word `delivered`. The three receipt tests the plan filed under `mailbox::` live in `recursion_e2e`, where a faux child with a held-open turn already exists. growth +462: this stage alone, measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded (the stack of ten stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D214, stands at +12675 over main), 77 lines in yi-types against 80 estimated and 385 in yi-runtime against 260 (the payload parser with its kind and direction checks, the waiter's retirement guard and the reaped-child inbox were not in the estimate); weighed for deletion and taken: `route` and `deliver_to_child` were rewritten in place rather than wrapped, `message_params` and the string envelope helpers are gone, `rlm.wait` and the request share one timeout reader, the presented transcript entry stands in for a separate `presented_at` record, and the three receipt tests reuse the `recursion_e2e` harness instead of a second faux child in `mailbox.rs`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |

| 0.272.0 | 2026-09-20 | `fetch` pages: a capped read names the next offset (D213, extends D164; Closes #456). This is stage F1c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11). The `fetch` host request takes `offset` and `limit`, and a paged reply carries `next_offset`, null at the end: bytes of a `local://` text, entries of a `history://` listing, chars of another member's `kernel://` repr, which is how a repr past the 8,192-char cap is read at all. A request that names neither key is answered key for key as before (`fetch_session::a_read_that_names_no_page_replies_exactly_as_it_did_before_paging` holds the reply's exact JSON), so no caller, tool description or prompt byte moves and the request budget is untouched. Edges, each decided and tested: a zero, negative, fractional or non-numeric `offset` or `limit` is refused at the host boundary and never clamped; a huge limit means the rest, except that a `kernel://` page is clamped to the read cell's `VARIABLE_MAX_CHARS` and names the next offset; an offset past the end is an empty last page with a null `next_offset`, so a loop on `next_offset` ends; a byte offset is the caller's arithmetic and can land inside a UTF-8 sequence, so both cuts move to a char boundary and the end always passes the offset (`an_offset_inside_a_character_still_serves_text_and_always_advances` tries every offset of a text with two, three and four byte characters at limits one to five: every page is text, every `next_offset` advances, and from zero the pages rebuild the file exactly). A page is refused where nothing stable counts it: on every other scheme, on one `history://` entry, and on `history://<agent>/tail/N`, a window anchored at the end of a growing listing, so any append slides it; the whole listing and `since/<seq>` are anchored at the start and page cleanly, and a paged read of the reading session's own history appends no fetch-log row (`FetchLog::remember`), so a walk of it ends at any limit instead of chasing the rows each page wrote (`fetch_session::a_walk_of_this_sessions_own_history_ends_at_a_limit_of_one`). The page's hash and its fetch-log row are the page's own; the log keeps one row per address and every reader of it is a membership test. `rlm.fetch(url, offset=, limit=)` returns `rlm.Page`, a `str` with `next_offset`, and sends only the keys it was given; a paged `kernel://` read is text, never the dilled object. `Resolver::fetch` is `fetch_page(url, None)`, `KernelVariables::read` and `KernelService::read_variable` take the page, and the reply is built by `Fetched::into_reply` so the unpaged shape is testable. Tests: `fetch_session::a_capped_read_names_the_next_offset_and_the_next_page_continues_it` (T1) and three edge tests beside it, the live-kernel page read inside `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_fetches_its_own_variables_and_runs_bash_handles` (T2), and `test_rlm` for the payload. Reconciled against the plan: `roles.verify_quotes` still reads the whole page, because the digest it pins is the whole page's sha256 and a page's digest is not interchangeable with it in a stored quote; reading only the line's neighbourhood needs a digest rule of its own and is left as a follow-up the plan now names. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.271.0 | 2026-09-20 | Two shapes ship as library schedulers under the engine's admission and step table: fork_join over isolated writers and readers, and scatter with readers bound to partitions and quotes verified against the archive; four more are specified with their prerequisites (D212, extends D211; Closes #455). This is stage F1b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8.5, 8.6 and 11). `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/shapes.py` (stdlib only): both shapes are module-level `async def`s over `(plan, run)`, so the lease's identity check attaches a second `plan.run(shape=fork_join)` to the first, and their levers are module constants (`MAX_RESTARTS` 3, `RESTART_WINDOW` 600 s, `SCATTER_MAX_ROUNDS` 3). Each checks geometry before its first `start` and raises `Geometry(problems)` naming every problem at once: fork_join wants at least two todos that can run side by side, each a `Writer` in its own worktree or a `Reader`, and refuses a writer or an inline todo in the shared workspace; scatter wants two or more readers on disjoint partitions (a url inside another's directory counts as shared), each with a `schema` item so its answer is stored, and exactly one pending inline lead. One loop serves both: start what is ready in plan order, stop at a start refused for `admission` and ask again after the next settle with nothing behind it tried first, settle, repeat. fork_join restarts one_for_one: a todo that ended `failed` is retried alone, at most `MAX_RESTARTS` times within `RESTART_WINDOW` across the shape, after which nothing new starts and what runs is settled; a `retry` the engine refuses (`retries_exhausted`, `illegal_step`) is final and stays in `run.refusals`, and a blocked todo is never retried because only `failed` is. scatter runs rounds: the readers answer `shapes.ANSWER` (`{answer, quotes}`, a null answer abstains), an abstention, a failed reader and an answer with no surviving quote are dropped, and the lead, `async def lead(answers, number)` declared as the plan's one inline todo, sees only `{reader, answer, quotes}` and returns `{"commit": answer}` or `{"ask": question}`; a commit is submitted as the lead todo's product `{answer, rounds}` and goes through `done`, a later round declares `<reader>-r<n>` todos with the reader's own delegation and contract, the question as the note and the key alone as the label (a `TodoLabel` is eighty characters and holds no newline, and the lead writes the question, so any question a real lead would ask refused the round-two declaration until the review caught it; the fake host holds the label rule now), and the last round without a commit fails the lead with the cause. `roles.verify_quotes` fetches each cited url once through `rlm.fetch`, keeps a quote whose text is on its line inside the reader's own partition, and pins the sha256 it was read at; provenance, not entailment. The partition arm is what makes the disjointness geometry mean anything after the first start: the reader's wall is cooperative (section 7.6) and the owner fetches with its own reach, so a quote citing another partition, or a page outside the archive, would otherwise verify. The walkthrough fixtures are programs under `python/yi_runtime/tests/programs` (`typo-fix`, `arc-game`, `campaign`), and `plan_walkthrough::a_program_and_its_json_fixture_reach_the_same_plan_json` runs each as a cell of a real kernel beside its JSON twin on a second engine and compares every plan's state and version, its todos in order with their states, edges, attempts, retries, whether they are delegated and what a done one output, and the committed transitions in journal order, while `refusals`, `touched`, cause and note text, and a delegation's or contract's contents are not compared; `kernel_data_surface::both_shapes_schedule_under_the_real_admission_and_step_table` runs both shapes against the real `admit`, step table, verifier and resolver with a width of one, where the journal shows each refused start asked again and never overtaken, a reader whose quotes cite a wrong line and another partition is dropped before the lead, and a reader whose child failed is retried and dropped, the two legal steps from `failed` to `abandoned`. That journey found two faults the fake host could not: the host's schema subset has no union type, so `ANSWER` leaves `answer` untyped, and a bare string product is stored as text a `schema` item finds no JSON in, so the commit is an object. `Todo.decompose` looked a sibling edge up in the parent plan and raised on its own docstring example; it resolves edges among its batch now. Overhead on the fake host, in host requests: fork_join over two writers 9 against 4 sent by hand, scatter over two readers and a lead 18 against 2 direct fetches; every extra request is a read or a wait. Reconciled against the plan: the section 6.3 cap counts refused verdicts per attempt (`done.rs` `refused_verdicts`) and the scheduler sends one `done` per attempt, so the durable bound on a retrying shape is `RETRY_CAP`, and section 6.3 step 6, which read as though the lifetime `todo.refusals` counter were the cap, now says the per-attempt rule that `plan_ops::a_retry_opens_a_fresh_refusal_count_so_only_retry_cap_bounds_a_scheduler` pins; the lead is a function, not an object with `commit`; `verify_quotes` reads the line from the whole url because the host's fragment needs a tag, which F1c's paged `fetch` is what makes cheap on a large page; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix; two controls were added (the real-engine journey and the counted overhead). Not built: a writer child as lead, `rest_for_one`, `one_for_all`, the four later shapes, and the paired T3 row against direct retrieval, which is user-run. growth +12055: the stack of seven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D212, of which this one adds no Rust `src` line (Python is outside the measure); weighed for deletion and taken: fork_join and scatter share one scheduling loop, the lead protocol is a return value rather than a class, and no restart strategy with a single caller got a type. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: `fork_join` counted a `done` todo as one of the two that run side by side; it now counts ready and running todos only, so a rerun needs two arms left to run (Refs #455). Test: `test_yi_shapes::test_a_done_arm_is_not_one_of_the_two_that_run_side_by_side`; the one-for-one rerun declares a third writer. |
| 0.270.0 | 2026-09-20 | Plans are programs: the `yi` library opens, attaches to or resumes a plan, declares idempotent todos with contracts, runs one scheduler under a lease, and records its cells as an audit artifact (D211, extends D166; Closes #454). This is stage F1a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8 and 11), the first user-space stage: F0 made the plan engine one host request with a journal, verified completion and worktree acceptance, and the kernel could drive it only through raw `rlm.plan_op` calls that answered in rendered text. Library (`python/yi_runtime/src/yi`, in the wheel the kernel already installs, stdlib only): `Plan.create(goal, request_id)` never adopts a plan by its text and a retried create is the same plan; `Plan.attach` reads a plan as it stands; `Plan.resume` reads durable state and the host's reconciliation findings, never saved source, and lists in `plan.unresolved` every running attempt nobody can vouch for, which the scheduler never retries, fails or restarts. `plan.todo(key, label, after, delegate, accept, run)` is idempotent by key: the same declaration writes nothing, a changed one raises `SpecDrift(key, diff)` and writes nothing, and the append carries the revision it compared against. `Todo` holds the ops (`start`, `submit`, `done` raising `Refused(verdict)` or `Stale`, `fail`, `block`, `unblock`, `retry`, `decompose`, `result`, which keeps waiting on a child that asked you something instead of raising, `cancel`); `contract`, `cmd`, `schema` and `example` build the yi-types contract with every criterion frozen by content when the todo is declared; `Writer` and `Reader` build the delegation and its wall. `plan.run(shape, budget, detach)` is one scheduler per plan under a lease the owner kernel holds: the same shape attaches, another raises `RunActive`, and `Run` ends `verified_success`, `accepted_by_user`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `unresolved` (a completion no contract verified is never reported as verified). The default shape starts what the host says is ready and retries an admission refusal after a slot frees; `Run.launch`, `Run.settle` and `Run.over` are the seam F1b's shapes drive. A child the host calls `stuck` is waited on, not failed, and one it cannot vouch for (`needs_you`, or a name `rlm.wait` no longer lists) blocks its todo on the user rather than leaving the scheduler to drop it; a finished child whose contract has a `schema` item has its answer stored as the product; a `done` refused by a verdict on the product fails the attempt with the verdict in the cause, while an abstention or an escalation leaves the attempt for a decision, since neither judged the product (section 6.3). Host: `Op::Program { cell_id, source_ref }` (`crates/runtime/src/plan/program.rs`) journals a cell's source, read back from the artifact store before the record names it, carries the sha256 `program.py` has once that cell is appended, appends `# --- cell <id> <iso-time>` and the source to `program.py` after the commit (the next record appends any cell a crash left out; nothing rewrites the file and nothing executes it), records a cell id once, and is legal on a done plan so a `retry` there can be recorded first. The library takes the cell text from IPython's `pre_run_cell` hook, which the kernel prelude installs by importing `yi`, and sends the record before the cell's first mutating op on each plan. `plan.op` now answers with the typed `plan` and `notices` beside the text, names the engine's refusal `kind` and a refused `done`'s `verdict`, and takes `artifacts` (`{media_type, text}` blobs, owner only, at most thirty-four of one MiB, stored under the store's own digest before the op applies), because until now only a test could put a criterion into a plan; `plan://<id>/artifacts/<sha256>` serves a stored blob, which is the host-minted address of an inline product. `rlm.plan_op` passes `artifacts` through, and the venv identity hashes every package under the wheel's `src`, not `rlm` alone, so an edit to `yi` rebuilds the venv (`crates/kernel/src/bootstrap.rs`). Gates: `help(yi)` is the documentation, `test_yi_help` checks every name in `yi.__all__` and every public method for a last-paragraph example whose names exist and whose awaits match each callee's kind, `ext_e2e::fragment_examples_name_real_kernel_apis` knows `yi.` and `plan.` and refuses a name that is gone, and `check_prompt_examples.py` derives its modules from `python/yi_runtime/src/*/__init__.py` and treats every `yi` class as a handle. No prompt bytes moved. Reconciled against the plan: a todo's key lives in its label (`key: label text`) because `TodoSpec` has no field for it and thirty-five construction sites; a builder's `local://` input is frozen at declaration, not at `start`; `programHash` is set on the `program` record alone; the scheduler lease is kernel-local until F2b puts leases in the store; the T0 source-record test is `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect` because `store.rs` sits at its cap; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix. Not built: a marker for a recorded cell that later raised, a token budget, and `shapes`, `mail`, `judge` and `recipes/`, which are F1b, F2a and F3a. growth +12055: the stack of six stages, D192 to D195, D210 and D211, of which this one is +346 (`plan/program.rs` at 181, the typed reply and the artifacts rider in `plan/request.rs` at 94, the artifact route in `fetch/schemes.rs`, and the op's arms); weighed for deletion and taken: the T0 test moved out of `src` into `tests/plan_program.rs`, a `key` field on `TodoSpec` and a second host request for artifacts were not added, and the library re-implements no admission, step or verdict rule. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: the export found a cell on disk by searching all of `program.py` for its marker, so a source that quoted a later cell's marker line kept that cell out of the file while `program_hash` counted it; `export` now walks the file cell by cell over each cell's own bytes (Refs #454). Test: `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect` records a cell whose source quotes the next cell's marker line. |
| 0.269.0 | 2026-09-20 | A child's removal, abort and every other exit publish one terminal update; the TUI and console reconcile against the roster, survive bus lag and no longer hold finished cards behind a live cell (D210, extends D165; Closes #453). This is stage F1e of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11), a bug fix from the child lifecycle audit that lands on its own evidence. A child's lifecycle lived in the host's record map, the event bus and each client's cache with no shared transition: `SubagentHost::delete` and `SubagentHost::reap` took the record away without a publish, and `run_child` then found no record and skipped its own terminal update, so a TUI card frozen at `Running` was never committed and never dropped, and `rlm.delete_subagent` before a child's first event minted one such card per call. Host: `SubagentHost::retire` (`crates/runtime/src/mailbox.rs`) is now the one way a record leaves the roster; `delete` and `reap` both call it, so `reap` gains `delete`'s refusal of a worktree with no recorded disposition, and the accepting road of `done` marks the published branch retained so the reap it runs is not refused, and the final `ChildUpdate` is built from the removed record with a cut-short run forced to `Error` and the cause `interrupted`. `run_child` judges a run by its last assistant message, maps an aborted stop to the same cause, tells the parent `[subagent … interrupted]` instead of `finished`, and once the record is gone publishes, notifies and bills nothing, because `retire` already spoke. A run carries the interrupt epoch read when it was requested (`AgentSession::prompt_requested`, `abort_epoch`), so an abort fired before admission is no longer cleared and a child deleted before its first poll never starts. `spawn` reserves the name and the slot under the roster lock and builds the child outside it (`crates/runtime/src/subagent/build.rs`), so `states` and `list` answer during a slow build; the host's watch re-reads its counters from the session after a lagged bus. Clients: `App::sync_children` ends a running card the roster no longer lists as `gone` and commits it; both TUI forwarders are one `forward` (`crates/tui/src/app/bridge.rs`) that reports a lagged bus as a notice and keeps going, as the ACP forwarder does; a finished card waits only on the kernel cell it was born under, so a lost cell end cannot hold every card; a card adopted after the first tool event names that tool from the child's transcript instead of drawing `starting`, and the record's answer preview outranks a folded stream that lost events. The console row cache evicts its oldest finished row at thirty-two rather than dropping the new one, and gives no row to a child first heard of at its end. Kernel side: `RLMSpawnHandle.result` raises on a `stuck` child with its note, `rlm.delete_subagent` takes the handle `rlm.run` returns, and the wait it passes the host is clamped to the asked timeout (a float ulp past it failed `test_result_uses_wait_and_keeps_its_errors` about one run in six). Reconciled against the plan: `retire` publishes for every removal and `run_child` stays silent on a missing record, which is what makes a child's own exit exactly one update, a removal after that exit repeating it rather than contradicting it; the console cannot tell a retired child from a retained one without a protocol change, so its rule is the first-seen-finished one above; the commit gate keeps the born-under ordering the `a_child_that_finishes_inside_its_spawning_cell_lands_under_it` pair test defends. The `deadline` road of the exit test is not built: a session deadline ends the turn after it settles (`should_stop_after_turn`), so a child that runs out of clock leaves by the `complete` road already covered, and `set_deadline` is crate-private. growth +11709: the stack of five stages, D192 to D195 and D210, of which this one is +344 (`subagent/build.rs` at 95, `retire`, the TUI's `app/children.rs` and `app/bridge.rs`, which are mostly moved out of `app.rs` to clear its cap, and the console row rule with its unit test); weighed for deletion and taken: the `list_subagents` poll was already gone at F0a, the TUI's two forwarder loops became one, and `delete` and `reap` lost their private removals. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.268.0 | 2026-09-14 | A worktree todo is done only when its candidate and its integration both pass and the acceptance is recorded; every other exit records a disposition and never merges to free a slot; a stuck child is reported once per episode (D195; Closes #426). This is stage F0d of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11, sections 6.6, 7.5 and 7.6). `crates/runtime/src/plan/acceptance.rs` is the integration coordinator: the child's `submit` settles its lane quiescent as the candidate, verifies that commit in a checkout from the verification reserve, prepares the merge in a staging lane under the integration lock and verifies that exact tree; the owner's `done` is legal only once the journal proves a verified integration, publishes under the generation check and records `accepted` with the transition; a moved parent records `integration_stale` and is prepared again, on this call or the next `done`. `crates/runtime/src/lane/settle.rs` carries `Lane::candidate` and `Lane::settle`, `prepare` and `publish`: a fast-forward when git can, else the ref alone with the checkout following on every path the user did not touch, never over the user's own file in an untracked directory, always from the repository root, and never on a fast-forward failure that is not the user's dirt. `fail`, `drop`, `supersede` and the reap record a disposition before the lane and slot go, a lane that cannot settle stays held on its record, and `delete` refuses a worktree without one; a second `submit` of one token shares its verification as `done` does; the probe loop reports a stuck child once per episode. The forge issue for the stage is opened at land time. Review fix (Refs #426): a run killed between the publication and the `accepted` record left HEAD at the integrated commit, which the next `done` read as a moved parent and prepared again; a parent already at the integrated commit now proceeds to the acceptance record with `how` null (`lanes::accept::a_publication_killed_before_its_record_is_accepted_by_the_next_done`); and the staging session is journaled as `integration_intent` before `prepare` claims its lane, so the next preparation frees the slot and deletes the branch a killed run left and records that on its own intent (`lanes::accept::a_staging_lane_killed_before_its_record_is_dropped_by_the_next_done`). growth +11365: `plan/acceptance.rs`, `lane/settle.rs`, `plan/capacity.rs`, the typed acceptance records in `crates/types/src/plan/acceptance.rs` and the stuck notice, measured against the 0.259.0 `src_loc.json` baseline main still carries, so the four stages' own +10572 and main's unabsorbed +793 ride inside it (D195); weighed for deletion, the B11 `merge_worktree` hand-back, kept: F0e did not measure it, so it stays the road for `rlm.run` worktree children, and it refuses a plan-dispatched child (`take_settled_worktree`), whose worktree lands only through this acceptance, which the engine drives from D225 (#483). |
| 0.267.0 | 2026-09-14 | `done` runs the todo's contract in the kernel against a frozen attempt and refuses with a recorded verdict; `set`, `import`, `repair` and `supersede` go through the same validator; a critical abstention cannot pass (D194, retires D77's fields; Closes #425). This is stage F0c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11, sections 6.1 to 6.5). `crates/types/src/plan/contract.rs` is the contract type: an item list of `cmd`, `schema` and `example` deciders with a class floor, weights, a threshold and a coverage floor, aggregated by section 6.2, and a `judge` item refused at declaration until F3a. `Todo.contract` and `Todo.attempt` land on the document; D77's ladder fields are gone and old records park them in `extra`. `plan/verify.rs` is the verifier: `start` freezes the contract and its criteria by digest, `done` commits `verification_requested { token, effect_id, claim }` under the lease, releases it, runs every item under one whole-verification deadline that also reads the session deadline of D177 and per-item deadlines (`/bin/sh -c` by absolute path, `env_clear()` plus the manifest's declared names, its own process group killed as a tree, capped output, every `protected` path digested before and after the run), re-acquires the lease, compares the whole token and commits either the verdict with `Done { VerifiedDone }` as one record or `done_refused { verdict }`; a moved token commits `verification_stale` and charges nothing; the third refused verdict on one attempt steps the todo `Blocked { on: User }` as a recorded transition. A verdict settles the effect its record names; concurrent `done` calls for one token join the flight registered under the lease, another process's live claim is refused until it dies or overruns, and an effect another process settled is replayed rather than charged again. | 0.266.0 | 2026-09-13 | The plan store is an append-only journal with a typed JSON checkpoint and artifact blobs; recovery reconstructs without effects and reconciles the rest; hand edits are gone and a user's administrative ops are confirmed in the session (D193, amends D97 and D105; carries D26 and D53; Closes #424). Tests: the crash matrix in `plan_journal.rs` (injected write and sync failures, a real kill-9 child, the opening ops), `plan_recovery.rs` (no effects on repair, the ambiguous spawn, damage at record 1 and in the middle, the torn tail, the detached checkpoint, the missing sub-plan), `plan_import.rs` (every byte kept, a skipped release, the retry, the lost checkpoint), `plan_e2e::a_reused_request_id_is_refused_by_the_journal_and_a_bad_one_by_the_parser`, `plan_ops::a_user_op_is_recorded_with_its_user_citation`, `acp_protocol::a_submit_over_the_worker_cannot_confirm_an_administrative_op`, `plan_ops::agent_cli_cannot_reset_fuse_as_user`, `rpc_protocol::a_socket_client_cannot_confirm_an_administrative_op`. The second review round fixed: a reused `request_id` is refused across op kinds, not only across args (`plan_journal::a_reused_request_id_with_another_op_kind_is_refused`); a confirmed op binds the root the human saw; a failed first append removes the empty journal it created, so the store is not wedged (`plan_journal::a_failed_first_append_leaves_no_journal_behind`); an imported graph runs the domain validator before its genesis record (`plan_import::an_invalid_graph_is_refused_before_the_genesis_record`); the artifact directory is synced after the blob rename; `yi plan import <id>` and `yi plan import local://<path>` parse as the store prints them (`plan_ops::the_import_commands_the_store_prints_run_as_printed`); `load` reads the journal once. growth +3939: the journal, its reducer and recovery, explicit import with artifact blobs and the confirmed user path, measured for D193; the Markdown writer and the hand-edit fold were deleted for them, and the plan skill's Python went with F0a. Round 3 of the review: `admit` is the engine's own refusal on `start` (`PlanOpError::Admission` with the count, before any spawn intent; `admissible` is deleted and the held list is derived from the same refusals); the record cap is rehearsed before the first reap with an 8 KiB envelope for the reaped urls, so an over-cap `supersede` kills nothing; effect ids carry the clock like request ids and the reducer refuses a duplicate `spawn_intent`; the session engine's liveness is the host's hold, so `repair` lists only children the host no longer holds; the bare `plan://<id>` address reads through the journal and regenerates a lost checkpoint; the fuzz lane gains `Import`. Pinned by `plan_ops::a_ninth_delegated_start_is_refused_by_the_engine_with_the_count`, `plan_ops::a_record_over_the_cap_is_refused_before_any_reap`, `plan_recovery::a_duplicate_spawn_intent_is_refused_by_the_reducer`, `plan::dispatch::tests::a_held_child_is_alive_and_an_unknown_one_is_not`, `fetch::schemes::tests::a_format_two_plan_is_served_from_its_journal_not_its_checkpoint`. |

This is stage F0a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11), which closes gaps 2 and 3 of that plan's section 1. Gap 3: plan mutations reached the engine only through the JSON tool, so a program running in the kernel could not move a todo, and the kernel-side skill that said it could called four names nothing registers. Gap 2: a child's terminal notice rode a hook that queues a steer and starts no turn, while doctrine tells the model to block on the child and end the turn, so an obedient parent sat idle until the user spoke, and the shared pending counter behind `wait` had the matching defect. `plan.op` (`crates/runtime/src/plan/request.rs`, new) takes `{request_id, plan?, expected_revision?, op, args}`, shares `parse_op` and `PlanEngine::apply` with the JSON plan tool, fixes the actor from the registry it is registered on (`Owner` on the root kernel, `Child(name)` on a child's, the way the child link overwrites `agent_message.send`), compares `expected_revision` on the plan the op names, and answers a duplicate `request_id` from a 64-reply cache until the F0b journal takes duplicates over. The child host's `notice` now wraps `wake_idle_hook`, so a parent that ended its turn blocked on a child starts the next turn when the child finishes, and a notice arriving at the parent's turn end is not lost. `rlm.wait(timeout, cursor=None)` returns `{cursor, changed, states, notes}` from a family epoch every spawn, report, finish, interrupt and delete moves, so two waiters never steal each other's updates, a late waiter sees the terminal state at once, and a delete wakes a waiter with the child off `states`; `updated` stays one release. `RLMSpawnHandle.result` awaits `wait` with its own cursor instead of polling `list_subagents`, keeps its 540 s default and missing-child error, and collects a child that ended asking its parent (`needs_you`) instead of timing out; `rlm.plan_op` is the kernel-side call. `python/skills/plan/` and its `PYTHON_SKILLS` entry are gone; `skills/yi/plan/` stays. | 0.264.0 | 2026-09-19 | Yi reads where it runs and tells the model (D209, #444). growth +793: five stages, of which this one is +169 — the probe and its vendor tables. Most of those lines are the tables themselves, which were weighed against a metadata call and kept because a network probe is what a sandboxed session cannot do and what a cloud bills for. The environment block named the OS, the architecture and the shell, so a session could not tell a laptop from a Docker container, a VM, an ssh login or a cloud instance. The `platform:` line now ends with a host clause — `host local`, `host docker container, over ssh`, `host aws vm (Amazon EC2)` — and `yi doctor` carries a `host` row. Every probe is a file or an environment variable read once (container markers and cgroups, the ssh variables, WSL's osrelease, the DMI vendor, product and asset tag, the cpuinfo hypervisor flag), plus the one macOS sysctl; no metadata endpoint is called and no network is touched. Seen red: `host_facts::host_probe_reads_container_vm_cloud_ssh_and_wsl` and `host_facts::a_bare_host_reads_local` (the module did not exist; with a stub probe every fixture read as `local`), `environment::the_platform_line_names_the_host`, `cli_surfaces::doctor_reports_and_repairs_what_it_may` (no `host` row in the table or the JSON). |
| 0.263.0 | 2026-09-19 | The console status row names the lane the session runs in (D208, #443). growth +516: four stages, of which this one is +82 — a diffed watcher beside the goal watcher, one decode arm, one setter. The alternative weighed was sending the workdir on every event, which costs a frame per turn for a value that changes once a session. The worker now says where the session actually runs — `_yi/workdir` carries the lane path and its `<repo> ⎇ lane <slot>` label on attach and whenever it changes — and the console repoints the row and its branch. The daemon still routes by the launch root. | 0.262.0 | 2026-09-19 | "Always allow" keeps a directory or a subcommand, not one exact call (D207, #442). growth +434: three stages together, of which this one is +273 — the grant reader and its two identities in `yi_permission::rules`, and the option list each surface now builds instead of a constant. The narrower design was one always-option with no label, which is what shipped before and is what the incident is: a grant nobody can see the scope of. Deleting the static `OPTIONS` paid part of it back; `CallOutcome::identity` and the `denied` argument went with D206, which is where the memory that read them was replaced. A structured tool's session rule was keyed on its whole arguments JSON, patch body and all, so "Allow always" on an edit never matched the next edit and the user was asked again for every file; a command's rule was keyed on its exact text. The prompt now offers what the grant would cover: `Always allow edits under crates/tui/src`, `Always allow edits anywhere in this tree`, or ``Always allow `git worktree` in this tree``, and keeps the one chosen. A grant is bounded by the tree its label names: a command is grantable only when its program is named on PATH, not by path, and carries no `-C`, `--git-dir` or `--work-tree`, so `git -C /elsewhere worktree remove --force` is not covered by a granted `git worktree`; a write at the tree root offers the exact call first, so `a` never hands over the repository. A later path write is allowed when every target sits under a granted directory inside the working tree; a later command when its single unproven program and verb is granted, and never when a segment is destructive, reaches the network, carries an env-prefixed argv0, or cannot be parsed. Catastrophic targets and configured denies still refuse. Seen red: `decide::a_scope_grant_keeps_the_promise_its_label_makes` (a granted `git worktree` allowed `git -C /home/user/other-repo worktree remove --force`, and a granted `python3` allowed `./python3`), `decide::a_root_level_edit_offers_the_exact_call_before_the_tree` (a README edit offered only the tree, and `a` then allowed every file in the repository), `permission_scope::always_allow_on_an_edit_does_not_ask_for_the_next_edit_in_that_dir` (two asks for two edits in one directory), `tui_drive::an_always_allowed_directory_edits_without_a_second_prompt` (the real binary asked for the second write and the drive never idled), `decide::a_directory_grant_allows_later_edits_beneath_it_only`, `decide::a_scope_grant_allows_the_same_subcommand_with_other_arguments`, `decide::grants_offer_the_target_dir_then_the_tree_root`, `tui_e2e::an_approval_offers_one_always_option_per_grant` (one unnamed `Allow always` row), `acp_unit::permission_bridge_writes_the_request_and_maps_the_selected_outcome` (`allow_always_1` read as a rejection), `port::option_for_picks_the_nth_always_option_and_labels_it`. |
| 0.261.0 | 2026-09-15 | A command the sandbox refused asks the next time, whatever else the retry adds (D206, #441). growth +234: the scope reader and the grant's own bounds, over both stages — the git-dir reader (D205) and this one, each a dozen lines of file reads that replace a git spawn and an exact-text memory; the lenient splitter was weighed against reusing `parse` alone and earns its lines by escalating what the parser refuses to read. On the 2026-09-14 session the sandbox refused `git worktree add`, the hint promised that running the same command again would ask, and the model's retry appended `&& git status --porcelain \| wc -l`; the refusal was remembered by exact text, so the retry was contained again and no question ever surfaced. Now a refusal is remembered by the program and verb it ran (`git worktree`, `cargo fmt`, `mkdir`), any later contained command using one of them asks, and the hint says exactly that: "`mkdir` now needs permission: the next call using it asks instead of running contained, and is refused where nobody can answer". Seen red: `tui_drive::a_reshaped_retry_of_a_refused_program_reaches_the_approval_view` (the real binary contained the reshaped retry and never drew the approval view), `sandbox_seam::a_refused_program_asks_even_when_the_retry_text_differs` (the second `mkdir … && ls` was contained, not asked), `safety::scope_names_program_and_subcommand` (no scopes), `sandbox::a_denial_is_only_claimed_when_the_output_says_so` (the hint named no scope and promised a byte-identical rerun). |
| 0.260.0 | 2026-09-15 | Git runs inside the sandbox (D205, #440). On the 2026-09-14 session that fixed `/new`, the model worked in a lane, a detached worktree whose index and refs live in the trunk's `.git`; the sandbox wrote only the lane and tmp, so `git add` failed on `.git/worktrees/yi-auto-pool/index.lock` and `git worktree add` on its ref lock, and the user ran git by hand. A contained command may now write the worktree's git dir and the common dir — except `hooks`, `config` and the `commondir`/`gitdir` pointers, which host git runs or reads back, and which a contained process must not be able to plant. Deleting either dir is denied in every mode, and the denial now reads through wrappers, so `nice -n 5 rm -rf .git` is refused like the bare spelling. A `gitdir:` pointer is believed only when it names a directory holding a `HEAD`, so repository data cannot make `/` a writable root. `git fetch`, `pull` and `push` ask because a contained run has no network, and `git -C <dir> reset --hard` reads as the destructive `reset` it is. A headless drive takes `--faux <cassette>` so a journey can script the model's tool calls. Seen red: `tui_drive::a_lane_session_commits_contained` (the real binary's contained commit in a lane never reached the object store), `sandbox::a_contained_git_commit_succeeds_in_a_linked_worktree` (`index.lock`: Operation not permitted), `sandbox::a_contained_command_cannot_write_a_hook_or_the_config` (the hook was written, and would have run on the host), `sandbox::a_linked_worktree_sandbox_writes_its_gitdir_and_common_dir`, `decide::git_dirs_follow_a_worktree_pointer_to_its_common_dir`, `decide::git_dirs_refuse_a_pointer_that_is_not_a_git_dir` (`gitdir: /` resolved to `/`), `decide::a_wrapped_destructive_command_is_denied_in_every_mode` (`nice -n 5 rm -rf <common>` only asked), `decide::rm_rf_of_a_linked_worktrees_common_dir_is_denied_in_every_mode`, `safety::git_network_verbs_ask_for_egress`, `safety::git_global_options_do_not_become_the_subcommand`. |

| 0.238.0 | 2026-09-12 | A timed-out command's descendants that left its process group die with it (amends D142; Refs #100). In the v4 sweep telecom-entity-resolution ran `cd /app && timeout 900 <venv>/bin/python solve.py` and read `[the process outlived the kill and keeps running as job 6; …]`: GNU timeout puts itself and its child in a new group, so the group kill reached the shell and left both holding the capture pipes. `kill_tree` now reads the process table before any signal (`/proc/<pid>/stat` on Linux, split right of comm's closing paren; `/bin/ps -eo pid=,ppid=` on macOS), collects every pid whose parent chain reaches the shell, and after the group and shell kills sends those pids one KILL through the shell builtin; the walk goes first because a re-grouped child reparents to init once its parent dies. A table that cannot be read, or a pid kill that cannot spawn, is named in `[group kill failed: …]` instead of reading as no descendants. One escape remains: a re-grouped descendant whose parent had already exited before the cancel is init's child, so the walk never reaches it and it runs to its own end; a subreaper or a cgroup would close that, and both need libc (§13.1). Tests: `jobs::tests::the_sweeps_timeout_python_chain_dies_at_the_bash_timeout` (the trial's `cd … && timeout … python … && ls` chain through the bash timeout; unfixed, it reads the sweep's `outlived the kill` line) and `process::tests::a_grandchild_that_left_the_group_dies_with_the_tree` (a `preexec_fn=os.setpgrp` grandchild, python 3.2 and later; unfixed, it held stdout for 30 s); both want a `ready` line first, so a missing `python3` fails instead of skipping. growth +204: measured at the push under D142; this version adds about 85 src lines and 57 test lines, and the other 62 are 0.235.0 to 0.237.0 against the 0.234.0 baseline. |

| 0.222.0 | 2026-09-10 | A continued session's kernel revives in the next process (amends D87; Refs #100). The root kernel's directory was its process's `rlm-<pid>`, so `--continue` looked for `<id>.kernel-state.dill` under the new pid, restored nothing, and a cell naming a variable from the replayed transcript raised NameError; the audit archive holds one bucket per process (`rlm-283/`, `rlm-288/`, `rlm-302/`) under one sessions directory. `RuntimeWiring::kernel_dir` is the sessions directory at depth 0, where `snapshot_paths` already keys the file by session id, and a child's own `sub-*` directory otherwise; the kernel's snapshot, `RLM_SESSION_DIR` and sandbox writable root all read it, so the root kernel writes where bash already could. The kernel side's local harness store follows `RLM_SESSION_DIR` to `sessions/harness/`, so it outlives the process and is one store for every session under that directory, in every project; an `rlm.harness` entry written in one project is read in the next. `rlm-<pid>` keeps the family directory, the schedule ledger and the children, so `main.rs` and the `serve_daemon` ledgers are untouched. A root whose store failed to open still boots unkeyed, and its `kernel-state.dill` is now shared with the next such run rather than kept per process. `/new` and RPC `new_session`, `switch_session` and `fork` swap the store under a live kernel, which kept writing under the first session's id; with the file now in the shared directory that would overwrite the first session's snapshot with the next one's names, so `KernelService` reboots a kernel whose snapshot path no longer matches the attached store, as it already did for a changed sandbox, and the new session starts from its own snapshot or none. | 0.221.0 | 2026-09-10 | A control byte no longer re-forms a fence header in the prompt yard (amends D98; Refs #100). Its nonce is wrong, but nothing reads a fence back except the model. `fetch` had fixed the same order in its own copy of the literals. One `pub(crate)` sanitizer in `ext/assemble.rs` now strips control characters first and escapes last, `fence_untrusted` calls it, and fetch's copy is deleted. A run of four or more `<` still leaves one `<<<` after the escape, `<<<<yi-external` becoming `<\<<<yi-external`, with or without a control byte; that predates this version and is left to a follow-up. | 0.220.0 | 2026-09-10 | A read is refused only at a key store, the workspace `.git` or a device (D180, amends D15; Refs #100). Every target went through the whole catastrophic list before the mode was read, so under `--yolo` a `read` of `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf` or `/usr/include/stdio.h` was denied while `bash cat` printed the same bytes. A call that only reads and reports itself reversible is now judged by `read_is_catastrophic`: the workspace `.git`, a path at or under a credential store in home, a directory above one (grep's walk of `/` or `~` would carry into `~/.ssh`), and `/dev`, where `/dev/zero` never ends and `/dev/tty` waits; every other call meets the whole list as before, and `command_reads_credentials` shares the credential-store list. `/etc`, `/usr`, `/var` and `/proc` become readable, `/proc/self/environ` included, which `bash env` already printed, but not a `/proc` path through a link (`/proc/self/root`, `cwd`, `fd`, `map_files`), which reaches a key or the `.git` by another name; `/`, `/home`, `/Users` and `/root` stay refused whatever HOME is, since other users' key stores sit under them. A tool's default `irreversible` now follows the call's own kind, so grep's replace+apply, which the gate took for a read, keeps the whole list, asks in ask mode, and in auto runs unasked only on a `path` inside the working tree. The yolo fragment names what a read may not touch. | 0.219.0 | 2026-09-10 | A capped command stream keeps its end as well as its start (amends D161; Refs #100). `drain_capped` kept each stream's first 30,000 bytes and dropped the rest, so a `pytest -q` whose failures ran past that lost its `FAILED` lines and its `N failed, M passed` summary from the result, from D161's reduced view and from the file its `[full output: path]` pointer names, and a finished job's report showed the opposite end from its live polls. Each stream now keeps the first half and a rolling last half of its cap, with `[N bytes omitted from the middle]` between them; the counters saturate, each half is decoded on its own (a character split at a seam becomes U+FFFD), and a stream that fits is decoded whole. Every `run_captured` caller shares the change, so a document conversion's closing status line and a failed goal check's last lines also survive a long stream. The raw stream is still not teed: that needs the recovery directory threaded through `run_captured`, and the audit slice's 268 bash calls capped none. Tests: `tools::a_capped_bash_stream_keeps_the_tail_with_the_verdict` (a 43,923-byte stream keeps its first rows, its last line and the exact omitted count), `process::tests::an_uncut_stream_is_decoded_whole`. growth +14812: measured at the push under D161; this version adds about 20 src lines. |
| 0.218.0 | 2026-09-10 | The repeat breaker counts a batch over the last six turns and sees a text-only turn (D179, amends D152; Refs #100). D152 compared a turn only with the one before it and gave a turn with no tool call no signature, so `bash ls` and `todo view` taking turns, a call repeated between job polls and a reply repeated to an intercept that keeps re-driving were never counted: in the audit slice 147 of 1,044 turns repeated a batch, four of them back to back, and the breaker fired once, while cargo Lb45bGG spent 273 of its 444 turns and about $2.05 answering one intercept. Each turn's batch, or the text of a turn with no calls collapsed to single spaces and cut at 256 characters, now joins a window of the last six; a third copy in the window sends the hidden steer once the last three turns each repeated a batch already in the window, and the sixth, which must fill the window, ends the run, so two batches taking turns are steered once and never ended. The steer goes once per stretch, until a batch new to the window shows progress: two batches taking turns hold the count at three, and a steer on every such turn would itself keep two replies re-driving without end. A fresh edit before each run of one check is new to the window, so that loop is never steered, and an identical batch draws the steer at its fourth run rather than its third. A follow-up prompt starts an empty window, as it starts its own cut count. A job poll and a cut turn, which keeps no text, still count for nothing, and the message no longer claims the same result, which was never compared. Replayed over 253 archived sessions the rule sends 11 steers in 4 sessions where D152 sent 6 in 3, none with an edit in the five turns before, and would have ended Lb45bGG at turn 215; a steer at any third copy sent 31 in 15, and four of them landed on an edit and a check taking turns, two of them in v4 trials. Tests: `loop_events::an_alternating_pair_and_a_text_only_spiral_are_broken` (red: two calls taking turns for eight turns drew no steer, `left: 0`; the same reply with its whitespace varied, answering an intercept that always re-drives, draws one steer and ends at the sixth, and four cut turns draw none), `loop_events::a_follow_up_prompt_starts_its_own_repeat_window` (red: the same answer to a prompt and two follow-ups drew a steer, `left: 1`), `loop_events::an_edit_then_check_loop_is_not_steered` (red on a steer at any third copy: five fresh edits, each followed by the same `cargo test`, drew three steers, `left: 3`). growth +14795: measured at the push under D179; this version adds about 25 src lines. |
| 0.217.0 | 2026-09-10 | Reasoning cuts are counted per prompt, and a truncated call is a length strike (D178, amends D153 and D168; Refs #100). Any turn with a tool call zeroed both the cut and the length count, so D168's twelve cuts in a row was never approached: the audit slice's photonic trials were cut 22 times, never more than four in a row, while the cuts took two thirds of each trial's request wall. A tool call now leaves the cut count alone and the prompt's sixth cut ends the run, while a follow-up prompt queued behind it starts its own count; the re-drive that names the write follows the prompt's second cut. A length stop that carries a call still fails it and asks for it whole, and now counts toward the three length strikes instead of resetting them: a call past the 32,768-token output ceiling comes back truncated with new partial arguments, which neither the repeat breaker nor the reset count could end. Only a turn whose calls run resets the length count. One check after the turn covers repeats, length stops and cuts; it runs before the steering queue is read, so a bare length stop now counts even with a steer waiting, which it did not before. Tests: `loop_events::cut_turns_are_counted_per_prompt_not_per_tool_call` (red: six cuts between tool calls re-drove at rungs `[1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]` and ran on to the answer), `loop_events::a_length_stop_carrying_a_truncated_call_is_a_strike` (red: three truncated calls ran on to a fourth turn), `loop_events::a_follow_up_prompt_starts_its_own_cut_count` (red: a follow-up's first cut, after five in the prompt before it, ended the run). growth +14768: measured at the push under D178; this version adds two src lines. |
| 0.216.0 | 2026-09-10 | `--deadline` is enforced, not only shown (D177, amends D150; Refs #100). D150 put the clock in the environment block and nothing read it, so a command or a kernel cell that outlived the budget held its turn until harbor killed the container. The session now holds one `Deadline`, set once from the wiring, and three readers share it: the environment line; the tool cancel flag, which fires at zero the way Esc does, so bash's watchdog kills the process group and the kernel watcher interrupts the cell; and the loop's `should_stop_after_turn`, which ends the run after the turn in flight once 240 s, or a quarter of a shorter budget, is all that is left. The turn is never cut: it settles and `yi ask` exits 0. The margin is 240 s, not 120 s, because the longest request measured, a settled reasoning cut, ran about 170 s and one that starts inside the margin must finish before harbor's kill; a flat margin would be longer than the whole 120 s and 180 s budgets of six fixtures and end each of their runs after one turn. A child session starts its own clock from the same total, and a headless TUI drive's `--deadline` now bounds its session as well as its script. `yi ask` also read a lagged event stream as the end of the run and exited 0 mid-turn; it now prints the count to stderr and reads on. `run_serve_command` moves to shells.rs beside the other launchers, since main.rs sat at its 1,200-line ceiling. Tests: `session_faux::a_deadline_kills_a_running_bash_call` (red: a 1 s deadline left `sleep 30` running past 5 s), `session_faux::a_deadline_ends_the_run_after_the_turn_in_flight` (red: the second reply was requested after a 4.6 s call ran past a 6 s budget's margin), `session::deadline::tests::the_stop_margin_is_four_minutes_or_a_quarter_of_the_budget`. growth +14766: measured at the push under D177; this version adds about 90 src lines. |
| 0.215.0 | 2026-09-10 | A kernel cell has a wall clock, and an abandoned snapshot no longer refuses the next cell (D176; Refs #100). The ipython watcher's only exit was the interrupt, so a `while True:` loop, a blocking read or a `%%bash` cell held the turn until harbor killed the container. A cell is now interrupted at `cell_ceiling` on the kernel service's options, bash's 600 s ceiling unless set, on a clock that starts after the kernel boots, and the tool's description says so. The debounced snapshot's five-second abort forced its cell to `Aborted` after the grace but kept it in the active slot, so the user's next cell waited out the busy window and ended `BusyAfterInterrupt`, which restarts the kernel with at most its last finished snapshot; Yi's own cells now give the slot up, and a user cell keeps it for the busy-reuse recovery. Clearing the slot alone lost the next cell instead: ipykernel aborts every request queued behind a failed one, and Yi read the aborted request as an empty success, so internal cells go out with `stop_on_error` off. A `%%bash` cell comes back aborted at the ceiling, but its shell runs on: IPython's magic ends the cell on a `TypeError` without stopping it (a probe's child wrote its file after the abort), and 0.214.0's group kill covers the bash tool, not the kernel. A child handle's `result` waited 900 s by default and the doctrine and orchestrate examples waited 900 s in one cell, which the ceiling now cuts at 600 s before the child's answer or the documented `TimeoutError` arrives; the default is 540 s and the examples, which wait 120 s first in the same cell, collect with `timeout=420`. Tests: `prompts::no_prompt_example_waits_past_the_cell_ceiling` (red on the 900 s examples: `an example waits 1020 s in one cell`), `ipython_faux::a_cell_that_never_returns_is_aborted_at_the_ceiling` (a 1 s ceiling returns the output so far and `[cell aborted]`), `kernel_e2e::an_aborted_internal_cell_clears_the_active_slot` (an internal cell deaf to the interrupt for 10 s, then failing on it; the next cell returns `'alive'`). growth +14676: measured at the push under D176; this version adds about 20 src lines. |
| 0.214.0 | 2026-09-10 | A timed-out command's process group is killed without a `kill` binary (amends D142; Refs #100). `kill_tree` spawned `kill -KILL -- -<pgid>` through PATH and dropped the result; slim benchmark images ship no `kill(1)` (Debian's lives in `procps`), so the ENOENT went unseen and a timed-out `python3` outlived its shell and kept running as a job on three trials. The group kill now runs `/bin/sh -c 'kill -KILL "-$1"'`, the shell's builtin, in the one form dash and bash both parse (dash refuses `--` and `-s KILL`), and goes before the shell's own kill, since macOS answers EPERM for a group whose only member is a zombie, and not at all once the shell is reaped, when its pid may name another group; a kill that fails is named as `[group kill failed: …]` in the bash result, or in the job's late report when the surviving group held the pipes past the kill's five-second grace. The kernel's bootstrap lock and the plan store's lease probe their holder the same way, and a probe that cannot run no longer reads as a dead holder whose lock may be taken: `process_is_running` returns the error and `lock_is_stale` leaves the call to the lock's age, as for a lock without a pid. The lane warmer's stop probes and signals through the builtin too, and fails on a probe that cannot run instead of resetting the slot under a live install. Tests: `process::tests::a_process_group_dies_with_no_kill_binary_on_path` and `bootstrap_lock::a_live_holder_is_seen_with_no_kill_binary_on_path` (each reruns its own test binary under `PATH=/nonexistent` and wants it to report one test passed), `bootstrap_lock::a_probe_error_does_not_mark_a_lock_stale`, `jobs::tests::a_failed_group_kill_is_named_in_the_job_report`, `tools::bash_times_out_kills_the_command_and_says_how_to_raise_the_limit` (a timeout names no failed kill). growth +14658: measured at the push under D142; this version adds about 80 src lines. |
